Unifying Koszul dualities via point-set models Thanks: The first named author was supported by the National Science Foundation under Grant No. DMS-2424441, and a Wallenberg Scholar fellowship. The second named author was supported by the ANR project SHoCos ANR-22-CE40-0008. The third named author acknowledges the support of the ANR projects LieDG ANR-24-CE40-5367 and KAsH ANR-25-CE40-2861.
Dan Petersen, Victor Roca i Lucio, Sinan Yalin

Abstract
. The classical bar-cobar adjunction between dg algebras and dg coalgebras goes back to the origins of differential homological algebra as developed by Cartan, Eilenberg, Moore, and many others, and is part of the broader framework of Koszul duality . In recent years, several ∞ \infty -categorical analogues of this adjunction have been developed, notably by Lurie, Francis–Gaitsgory, and Heuts. However, there is no comparison in the literature between the classical chain-level constructions and their higher-categorical counterparts, and in fact the two constructions are not quite compatible. In this paper we provide a unified framework relating these different forms of Koszul duality in the differential graded setting. We construct a commutative square of adjunctions—called the inclusion-restriction square—intertwining the classical operadic bar-cobar adjunction with its completed variant due to Le Grignou–Lejay. We show that this square induces an ∞ \infty -categorical adjunction between algebras and their Koszul dual coalgebras, recovering in particular the differential graded case of Lurie’s bar-cobar adjunction, and explain precisely how our constructions relate to those of Francis–Gaitsgory and Heuts.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
18N70, 18M60, 18N40, 18N60, 55P48, 55P65, 55U15, 55U35, 16T15

Introduction

0.1.
Bar-cobar duality

A fundamental object of study in homological and homotopical algebra is the
bar-cobar adjunction

[
17
,
1
]
:

(1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\text{augmented dg algebras})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\text{conilpotent coaugmented dg coalgebras}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scriptstyle\perp}

These functors have their origin in algebraic topology — they are algebraic shadows of the formation of
classifying spaces
and
loop spaces
. If
M
is a topological monoid, then its chains
C_{*}(M;\mathbb{Z})
is an augmented dg algebra under Pontryagin product, and there is a quasi-isomorphism of dg coalgebras
[
55
,
45
]
:

\mathrm{B}C_{*}(M;\mathbb{Z})\simeq C_{*}(\mathrm{B}M;\mathbb{Z}).

Similarly if
X
is a connected based space, then
C_{*}(X;\mathbb{Z})
is a coaugmented dg coalgebra, and there is a quasi-isomorphism of dg algebras
[
1
,
51
]
:

\Omega C_{*}(X;\mathbb{Z})\simeq C_{*}(\Omega X;\mathbb{Z}).

These theorems justify the notations
\mathrm{B}
and
\Omega
for the bar and cobar functors. The topological analogue of (
1
) is the adjunction

(2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\text{topological monoids})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\text{based spaces}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scriptstyle\perp}

Up to homotopy, (
2
) is nearly an equivalence: it restricts to an equivalence of
\infty
-categories between group-like topological monoids and connected based spaces. Its algebraic shadow (
1
) is likewise an equivalence up to quasi-isomorphism, if one restricts to suitably connective objects. This almost-equivalence between the homotopy theories of algebras and coalgebras,
and a number of closely related algebraic dualities of a similar flavor, are often collectively referred to as
Koszul duality
.

In fact, there are a bewildering number of notions
1
1

1

To add to the confusion, some authors reserve the phrase “Koszul duality” only for a more restrictive notion involving
small resolutions
. This is also the only part of the story which relates to the work of Koszul, via the
Koszul complex
of
[
49
]
. A quadratic algebra
A
has a
quadratic dual
coalgebra
A^{!}
and a canonical map
A^{!}\to\mathrm{B}A
, and one says that
A
is a
Koszul algebra
if this canonical map is a quasi-isomorphism. In this case, its adjoint
\Omega A^{!}\to A
is also a quasi-isomorphism, and a minimal resolution of
A
.
While there are several generalizations of this version of Koszul duality to other algebraic objects, one always constructs first a suitable bar-cobar adjunction that establishes a
homotopical
Koszul duality between algebras and coalgebras, with respect to which the story about minimal resolutions becomes meaningful. The present paper is concerned only with the underlying homotopy-theoretic duality.

in the literature which by now are called Koszul duality. A full historical account is beyond the scope of this introduction, but we wish to mention
[
49
,
21
,
40
,
50
,
56
,
12
,
25
,
9
,
38
,
42
]
.

A more recent, less classical approach to the bar-cobar adjunction is due to Jacob Lurie
[
43
, Section 5.2.2]
. Let
\mathbf{C}
be a monoidal
\infty
-category which admits geometric realizations of simplicial objects and totalizations of cosimplicial objects. Under these assumptions, Lurie constructs an adjunction

(3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\text{augmented $\mathbb E_{1}$-algebras in $\mathbf{C}$})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\text{coaugmented $\mathbb E_{1}$-coalgebras in $\mathbf{C}$}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{bar}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{cobar}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scriptstyle\perp}

When we specialize
\mathbf{C}
to the
\infty
-category of spaces
\mathbf{Spc}
, the adjunction (
3
) specializes to (
2
). Indeed Lurie proves that
\mathbf{bar}(A)
can be computed as the realization of a simplicial object

(4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots\hskip 12.91663ptA\otimes A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{1}}

which gives back the familiar formula for the classifying space of a topological monoid. Note that every space is an
\mathbb E_{1}
-coalgebra in a unique way (with comultiplication the diagonal), and a coaugmentation is just a basepoint; also, every
\mathbb E_{1}
-algebra in spaces is automatically augmented, and can be rectified to a topological monoid.

Let us take
\mathbf{C}=\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
, the derived category of a ring
\Bbbk
. It would then be natural to think that for this choice of
\mathbf{C}
, the adjunction (
3
) specializes to (
1
). This is
not
the case. In fact, there are several immediate problems:

(i)

Lurie’s bar functor is
left
adjoint; the algebraic bar functor is
right
adjoint.

(ii)

Lurie does not impose conilpotence anywhere; algebraically, conilpotence is essential in order for
\mathrm{B}
and
\Omega
to be adjoints.

(iii)

The category of dg coalgebras localized at quasi-isomorphisms does not present the
\infty
-category of
\mathbb E_{1}
-coalgebras in the derived
\infty
-category of
\Bbbk
, no matter what ring
\Bbbk
we choose.

Nevertheless, (
4
) shows that Lurie’s
\mathbf{bar}(A)
is quasi-isomorphic to the classical algebraic bar construction
\mathrm{B}A
for any augmented dg algebra
A
. So, when
\mathbf{C}=\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
, if we consider the adjunction (
3
) in this case, there are a number of natural questions:

(a)

Can we construct explicit chain-level constructions of the functors
\mathbf{bar}
and
\mathbf{cobar}
?

(b)

What, if any, is the relationship between these chain-level constructions and the adjunction (
1
)?

(c)

Can we present (
3
) by a Quillen adjunction?

The goal of this paper is in particular to answer all of these questions, when
\Bbbk
is a field.

0.2.
The associative case

Let
\Bbbk
be a field. Before we can even meaningfully consider question
(a)
, we need to find suitable point-set models for
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-coalgebras in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
, which was the main goal of
[
48
]
. From now on we shall find it convenient to restrict our attention to non-unital algebras and coalgebras, rather than keeping track of augmentations and coaugmentations. In
[
48
, Theorem A]
, we showed that there exists an equivalence

\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbb{E}_{1}}^{\text{non-unital}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\simeq\mathcal{A}_{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]

between the
\infty
-categories of non-unital
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-coalgebras in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
, and (not necessarily conilpotent)
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-coalgebras up to quasi-isomorphism. Informally, this says that the reason for problem
(iii)
is that coassociative coalgebras are
too strict
to exhibit homotopically correct behavior, and need to be replaced by a flabbier notion.

Before proceeding further, let us dwell on the definitions of
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-algebra and
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-coalgebra. Usually, an
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-algebra is defined as a graded vector space
A
with a family of maps

\mu_{n}:A^{\otimes n}\longrightarrow A

of degree
n-2
, satisfying an infinite hierarchy of quadratic relations: these express that
\mu_{1}^{2}=0
, so that
(A,\mu_{1})
is a chain complex;
\mu_{2}
is a multiplication satisfying the Leibniz rule with respect to the differential
\mu_{1}
;
\mu_{3}
is a homotopy with respect to
\mu_{1}
between
\mu_{2}\circ(\mu_{2}\otimes\mathrm{id})
and
\mu_{2}\circ(\mathrm{id}\otimes\mu_{2})
, correcting the failure of
\mu_{2}
to be strictly associative, and so on. An efficient way to package all this data (and the signs involved) is to observe that the maps
\mu_{i}
determine, and are determined by, a coderivation
\bm{\mu}
of degree
-1
of the (reduced) tensor coalgebra
\bar{T}^{c}(sA)
on the suspension of
A
, since such a coderivation is freely determined by its composition with the projection to the cogenerators:

(5)

\bigoplus_{n\geq 1}(sA)^{\otimes n}=\bar{T}^{c}(sA)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bm{\mu}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\bar{T}^{c}(sA)\twoheadrightarrow sA.

In these terms, the infinite hierarchy of quadratic relations becomes
\bm{\mu}^{2}=0
. An advantage of this definition is that it becomes tautologically obvious how to extend the bar construction
\mathrm{B}
from associative algebras to
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-algebras: if
A
is an
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-algebra, then
\mathrm{B}A
is the dg coalgebra given by
\bar{T}^{c}(sA)
, with the differential determined by
\bm{\mu}
.

An
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-coalgebra can likewise be defined as a graded vector space
C
and a family of maps

\delta_{n}:C\longrightarrow C^{\otimes n}

of degree
n-2
, satisfying a dual hierarchy of quadratic relations. The dual statement of (
5
) is that any family of maps
\delta_{n}:C\longrightarrow C^{\otimes n}
uniquely determines a degree
-1
derivation
\bm{\delta}

(6)

s^{-1}C\hookrightarrow\bar{T}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}C)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bm{\delta}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\bar{T}^{\wedge}(s^{-1}C)=\prod_{n\geq 1}(s^{-1}C)^{\otimes n}

of the
completion
of the tensor algebra on the desuspension of
C
, since such a derivation is freely determined by where it sends the generators. This description makes it clear that the classical cobar functor
\Omega
for coassociative coalgebras (defined as in (
6
), but with a direct sum) does not extend to a well-defined functor on
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-coalgebras, but the
completed cobar functor
, which we shall denote
\widehat{\Omega}
,
does
extend in a tautological manner.

In fact, it will turn out that Lurie’s functor
\mathbf{cobar}
is given (on the chain level) by
\widehat{\Omega}
, the completion of the classical cobar functor. There are further reasons that
\smash{\widehat{\Omega}}
may be more natural than
\Omega
:

•

The classical cobar functor
\Omega
does not preserve quasi-isomorphisms, unless the coalgebras involved are suitable connective or coconnective. The reason is that
\Omega
sends quasi-isomorphisms to filtered quasi-isomorphisms
2
2

2

A filtered quasi-isomorphism is not in general a quasi-isomorphism!
, but only for a filtration which is generally not complete. Passing to the completion repairs this defect, and
\smash{\widehat{\Omega}}
always preserves quasi-isomorphisms.

•

Lurie’s
\mathbf{bar}
and
\mathbf{cobar}
are interchanged under switching
\mathbf{C}
and
\mathbf{C}^{\mathrm{op}}
. Then it is natural to expect that
\mathbf{cobar}
should be given by a formula dual to that for
\mathbf{bar}
, which entails switching
\bigoplus
for
\prod
.

The drawback of
\widehat{\Omega}
compared to
\Omega
is that it is in no sense an adjoint of the bar construction
\mathrm{B}
, working 1-categorically. This makes the following theorem surprising.

Theorem A
.

Let
\Bbbk
be a field of any characteristic. Let
\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathsf{assoc}\penalty\ \mathsf{alg}
and
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}
denote the relative categories of dg associative algebras, and
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-coalgebras, respectively, with weak equivalences given by quasi-isomorphisms. The functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathsf{assoc}\penalty\ \mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}}

are both relative functors, and hence induce functors between the respective localizations, which we continue to denote by the same name. Even though
\mathrm{B}
and
\smash{\widehat{\Omega}}
are not adjoints 1-categorically, the localized functors

(7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathsf{assoc}\penalty\ \mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

are adjoints, and (
7
) is equivalent to Lurie’s adjunction (
3
).

Let us explain how to understand the
unit
of the adjunction (
7
). Let
A
be an associative dg algebra. There exists a zig-zag

A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longleftarrow}}\Omega\mathrm{B}A\longrightarrow\widehat{\Omega}\mathrm{B}A

in which the first arrow is the
counit
of the classical adjunction (
1
), which is classically known to be a quasi-isomorphism for any
A
, and the second arrow comes from the fact that the ordinary cobar construction sits inside its completion. Working
\infty
-categorically, the first arrow is “invisible”, and it looks as if we just have a map
A\to\smash{\widehat{\Omega}}\mathrm{B}A
, which explains the reversal of handedness between (
1
) and (
7
).

The above description also identifies the unit with a derived functor:
A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longleftarrow}}\Omega\mathrm{B}A
is a cofibrant replacement of
A
, so the unit is given by first cofibrantly replacing and then taking the completion, which is precisely the
derived completion
(this notion has appeared in
[
29
,
16
,
53
]
). One can completely similarly describe the counit of the adjunction (
7
) as a zig-zag

C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}\widehat{\Omega}C\leftarrow\mathrm{B}\widehat{\Omega}C,

where
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}
is a non-conilpotent enlargement of the classical bar construction; we shall describe it further shortly. Now the first arrow is a
fibrant
replacement, and the similar consequence is that the counit of (
7
) is the derived functor of taking the maximal conilpotent subcoalgebra. We thus obtain:

Theorem B
.

The unit of the adjunction (
7
) is the derived completion functor, and the counit of the adjunction is the derived functor of taking the maximal conilpotent subcoalgebra.

B
is a special case of a more general theorem of Heuts
[
36
]
, but we see it here very explicitly. If we define
homotopy complete
dg algebras as those for which the unit of (
7
) is a quasi-isomorphism, and
homotopy cocomplete

\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-coalgebras as those for which the counit of (
7
) is a quasi-isomorphism, then (
7
) restricts tautologically to an equivalence of
\infty
-categories between homotopy complete associative algebras and homotopy cocomplete
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-coalgebras.

0.3.
The inclusion-restriction square

Our
A
answers, in a sense, both questions
(a)
and
(b)
, but not
(c)
. In fact, relative categories are an unwieldy model of homotopy theory, and it would be much preferable to have a description of Lurie’s bar-cobar adjunction as a Quillen adjunction. What we shall do is something almost as good: we will present it as a composite of
two
Quillen adjunctions, with opposite handedness.

To carry this out, we need to realize that there another 1-categorical bar-cobar adjunction, dual to (
1
), which relates (non-necessarily conilpotent) coalgebras and a new type of algebraic objects called
absolute algebras
. These are associative algebras where infinite sums of structural operations are well-defined
by definition
, without assuming an underlying topology. Absolute algebras are closely related to complete algebras, but subtly different. The free absolute algebra on a module
M
is however isomorphic to the completion of the free algebra on
M
. We refer to
[
27
,
53
]
for more details on these structures. There is a
complete bar-cobar adjunction

[
27
]
:

(8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{abs}\penalty\ \mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathsf{assoc}\penalty\ \mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\scriptstyle\perp}

between (non-necessarily conilpotent)
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-coalgebras and dg absolute associative algebras. Here the complete cobar functor
\smash{\widehat{\Omega}}
is defined as before. The complete bar functor is informally defined by replacing the tensor coalgebra used in the functor
\mathrm{B}
with the (genuine, non-conilpotent) cofree
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-coalgebra.

The final step is to combine the bar-cobar adjunction (
1
) with its dual completed version (
8
) to get the following
inclusion-restriction
square of adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathsf{assoc}\penalty\ \mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}^{\mathsf{conil}},}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{abs}\penalty\ \mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathsf{assoc}\penalty\ \mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}_{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c\mathrm{Abs}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sub}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Here the functor
\mathrm{Res}
restricts the structure maps of any absolute dg associative algebra to finite sums of operations, and thus gives its underlying associative algebra; it admits a left adjoint
c\mathrm{Abs}
. The functor
\mathrm{Inc}
includes conilpotent
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-coalgebras into all coalgebras, and it has a right adjoint
\mathrm{Sub}
.

Theorem C
.

The projective model structure on chain complexes may be right-transferred to a model structure on
\mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathsf{assoc}\penalty\ \mathsf{alg}
, and then further left-transferred along
\Omega
to a model structure on the category
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}^{\mathsf{conil}}
. Dually, the injective model structure on chain complexes may be left-transferred to a model structure on
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}
, and then further right-transferred to a model structure on the category
\mathsf{abs}\penalty\ \mathsf{dg}\penalty\ \mathsf{assoc}\penalty\ \mathsf{alg}
. With respect to these model structures, all the above adjunctions are Quillen adjunctions. The functor
\mathrm{B}\circ\mathrm{Inc}
is the functor denoted
\mathrm{B}
in
A
, and the functor
\mathrm{Res}\circ\widehat{\Omega}
is the functor denoted
\widehat{\Omega}
in
A
.

0.4.
Main results

The situation explained before is just an example of more general phenomena in the particular case of associative algebras. In the general context, we will work with a dg operad
\mathcal{P}
. We assume:

•

\mathcal{P}
is reduced,

•

\mathcal{P}
is non-symmetric or
\operatorname{char}(\Bbbk)=0
,

•

each
\mathcal{P}(n)
is a dualizable chain complex, that is, a complex of total finite dimension.

Given this data, we construct a general inclusion-restriction square

(9)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg},}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c\mathrm{Abs}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sub}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

The operadic bar-cobar adjunction
\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\vdash\Omega_{\pi}
, generalizing (
1
), is the one studied in
[
42
]
; its dual completed version
\smash{\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}\vdash\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}}
, generalizing (
8
), is studied in
[
27
]
.

Theorem D
.

The categories in the square (
9
) may be given transferred model structure in the same way as in
C
, making all adjunctions Quillen adjunctions. If we set

\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{P}}\coloneqq\mathrm{Inc}\circ\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\penalty\ ,\quad\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{P}}\coloneqq\mathrm{Res}\circ\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}\penalty\ ,

then there is an adjunction of
\infty
-categories

(10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between the
\infty
-category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras localized at quasi-isomorphisms and the
\infty
-category of dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras localized at quasi-isomorphisms. The resulting adjunction (
10
) coincides with the differential graded case of the adjunction considered by Heuts in
[
36
]
.

As in the discussion around
B
, we can define
homotopy complete

\mathcal{P}
-algebras as those which are fully faithfully included into absolute
\mathcal{P}
-algebras, that is,
\mathcal{P}
-algebras
A
such that the derived unit
A\longrightarrow\mathrm{Res}\circ\mathbb{L}c\mathrm{Abs}(A)

is a quasi-isomorphism. We can also define
homotopy cocomplete

\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras as those which are fully faithfully included into conilpotent
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras, that is,
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras
C
such that the derived counit

\mathrm{Inc}\circ\mathbb{R}\mathrm{Sub}(C)\longrightarrow C

is a quasi-isomorphism. As in
B
these are also the unit and counit of the
\infty
-categorical adjunction
\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{P}}\dashv\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{P}}
, and we obtain an equivalence of
\infty
-categories between homotopy complete
\mathcal{P}
-algebras and homotopy cocomplete
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras. This gives a new proof of
[
36
, Theorem 2.1]
in the special case that
\mathbf{C}=\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
, and moreover gives an explicit point-set description of each of these categories.

An important class of operad are operads
with good completions
, which are operads such that trivial algebras are homotopy complete. These have been introduced and studied by Heuts in
[
36
]
. In Subsection
6.3
, we give explicit criteria for an operad to have good completions in terms of our point-set descripions, and we also construct a first example of an operad without good completions. We also introduce the dual notion of a cofibrant operad
with good cocompletions
, that is, such that trivial coalgebras over it are homotopy cocomplete, and we study examples.

0.5.
Francis–Gaitsgory’s enhanced bar-cobar adjunction.

There is yet another bar-cobar adjunction that can be constructed at the
\infty
-categorical level
[
22
,
23
]
, and which is different from all those mentioned before. Let
\mathscr{P}
be an augmented
\infty
-operad enriched in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
. The augmentation
\mathscr{P}\longrightarrow\mathbb{1}
induces an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{indec}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{triv}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between the
\infty
-category of
\mathscr{P}
-algebras and the underlying
\infty
-category of chain complexes over
\Bbbk
. The functor
\mathbf{triv}
endows any chain complex with a trivial
\mathscr{P}
-algebra structure, and its left adjoint
\mathbf{indec}
is the functor of derived indecomposables of a
\mathscr{P}
-algebra. The
homology
of
\mathbf{indec}
is called the (topological) André–Quillen homology, see
[
6
,
44
]
. When
\mathscr{P}
is the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-operad in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
, the functor
\mathbf{indec}
is called the cotangent functor.

The point now is that this adjunction factors universally through coalgebras over the induced comonad
\mathbf{indec}\circ\mathbf{triv}
on
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
, and that these coalgebras can be identified with conilpotent
\mathrm{B}\mathscr{P}
-coalgebras. Therefore, we get an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P})}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\Omega}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

called the
enhanced bar-cobar adjunction
in
[
22
]
, between
\mathscr{P}
-algebras and conilpotent divided powers
\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P})
-coalgebras.

Naively, one would like to say that the top horizontal adjunction in (
9
) — which generalizes (
1
) — induces the enhanced bar-cobar adjunction at the
\infty
-categorical level. However, as pointed out in
[
18
, Theorem 5.3.1]
, this is not true in general. The main reason is that point-set conilpotent
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras up to quasi-isomorphisms are
not
equivalent to their
\infty
-categorical counterparts. What we get is the following diagram of adjunctions at the
\infty
-categorical level

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\Omega_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{loose}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{strict}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Sub}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathrm{Sub}}

where left adjoints are written on top. The composite horizontal adjunction does give back the enhanced bar-cobar adjunction
\mathbf{B}^{\mathbf{enh}}\dashv\bm{\Omega}^{\mathbf{enh}}
but, surprisingly, what happens is that the adjunction
\mathbf{loose}\dashv\mathbf{strict}
is not in general an equivalence. In
Section

6.4
, we construct counterexamples to the fully faithfulness of some of these functors, and we show however that the functor
\mathbf{loose}
is indeed fully faithful on the essential image of homotopy complete
\mathcal{P}
-algebras by the bar construction
\mathrm{B}_{\pi}
, see Corollary
6.25
. This puts together several partial rectification results for conilpotent coalgebras present in the literature, for example what happens when
\mathcal{P}
is connective and conilpotent
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras are in degrees
\geq 1
.

0.6.
A dual version of the enhanced bar-cobar adjunction.

In order to make sense of the bottom horizontal adjunction in (
9
) at the
\infty
-categorical level, we construct a dual version of the enhanced bar-cobar adjunction for non-conilpotent coalgebras. Let
\mathscr{P}
be an augmented
\infty
-operad enriched in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
. In the same way that we can use point-set operads to encode non-conilpotent coalgebras, the same happens with
\infty
-operads. We refer to
[
48
, Section 3]
for more details about this.

Here also, the augmentation map
\mathscr{P}\longrightarrow\mathbb{1}
induces an adjunction
\mathbf{triv}\dashv\mathbf{prim}
between the
\infty
-category of
\mathscr{P}
-coalgebras and the derived
\infty
-category of chain complexes over
\Bbbk
. The functor
\mathbf{triv}
endows any chain complex with a trivial
\mathscr{P}
-coalgebra structure and its right adjoint
\mathbf{prim}
is the functor of derived primitives of a
\mathscr{P}
-coalgebra. Its homology groups should be understood as (topological) coAndré-Quillen homology groups for types of coalgebras, of which (topological) coHochschild homology groups in the sense of
[
33
]
and
[
8
]
are particular examples. This adjunction also factors universally through algebras over the induced monad
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathscr{P}}=\mathbf{prim}\circ\mathbf{triv}
on
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
, giving the adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathscr{P}}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\widehat{\mathrm{B}}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

which we call the
complete enhanced bar-cobar adjunction
. However, unlike in
[
22
]
, it is not straightforward to identify what this monad is. In order to do so, we first give a definition (
1.34
) of algebras over an enriched
\infty
-cooperad — which are the
\infty
-categorical analogue of the absolute algebras considered in
[
53
]
. Then, we show that point-set algebras over a conilpotent dg cooperad up to quasi-isomorphisms do present their
\infty
-categorical counterparts in Corollary
4.8
. This combination of new
\infty
-categorical definitions and point-set methods allows to show the following result.

Theorem E

(Theorems
4.10
and
4.13
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent dg cooperad satisfying finiteness assumptions
1
. The monad
\mathbf{Q}_{\Omega\mathcal{C}}=\mathbf{prim}\circ\mathbf{triv}
induced by
\mathbf{triv}\dashv\mathbf{prim}
can be indentified with the monad

\widehat{\mathbf{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{C})\simeq\prod_{n\geq 0}\left[\mathcal{C}(n),(-)^{\otimes n}\right]^{h\mathbb{S}_{n}}

induced by the underlying enriched
\infty
-cooperad of
\mathcal{C}
, which encodes
\infty
-categorical
\mathcal{C}
-algebras. Furthermore, the
\infty
-categorical adjunction induced by the complete bar-cobar adjunction composed with the right Bousfield localization with respect to quasi-isomorphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{W}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Id}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is naturally weakly equivalent to the
\infty
-categorical complete enhanced bar-cobar adjunction.

0.7.
An
\infty
-categorical inclusion-restriction square

Finally, we put together all of our previous constructions at the
\infty
-categorical level to obtain another inclusion-restriction square.

Theorem F

(Theorem
5.6
)
.

Let
\mathscr{P}
be a reduced
\infty
-operad enriched in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
, with arity-wise bounded and finite dimensional homology groups. The following diagram of adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P})}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)),}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathscr{P}^{*}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{cobar}(\mathscr{P}^{*})}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathrm{B}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{cAbs}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\Omega}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\widehat{\mathrm{B}}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Sub}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

commutes in the following sense: left adjoints from the top left to the bottom right are naturally weakly equivalent.

Interestingly, while this commutative square does not coincide with the commutative square induced at the
\infty
-categorical level by (
9
), its commutativity does follow from the point-set methods that we have developed throughout this paper. And while we expect this square to hold in a much more general context than
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
, so far the only way we know to reach such a purely
\infty
-categorical statement about Koszul duality is via point-set methods.

0.8.
Acknowledgements

We wish to warmly thank Grégory Ginot for several discussions that led to this paper. The first author also wishes to thank Gijs Heuts and Brice Le Grignou for useful discusions on these topics.

0.9.
Conventions

Regarding notation, we will adopt the following conventions.

•

Let
\Bbbk
be a field, of any characteristic unless stated otherwise. Our base 1-category will be the category of chain complexes over
\Bbbk
, together with its closed symmetric monoidal structure given by the tensor product
-\otimes-
of chain complexes and the Koszul sign convention. We will denote the internal hom of this category by
[-,-]
. We adopt the
homological convention
, differentials will be of degree
-1
. We denote this 1-category by
\mathsf{Ch}(\Bbbk)
.

•

In general, dg operads in
\mathsf{Ch}(\Bbbk)
will be denoted by
\mathcal{P}
and dg cooperads by
\mathcal{C}
. For a dg operad
\mathcal{P}
, we denote the category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras by
\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}
and of dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras by
\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}
. Similarly for algebras and coalgebras over a dg cooperad.

•

Let
\mathsf{C}
be a category and let
\mathsf{W}
be a class of arrows in
\mathsf{C}
. We will denote
\mathsf{C}\penalty\ [\mathsf{W}^{-1}]
the
\infty
-category obtained by localizing
\mathsf{C}
at
\mathsf{W}
. When working at the
\infty
-categorical level, limits and colimits should be understood as meaning homotopy limits and colimits. If

\mathsf{F}:\mathsf{C}\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}

is a functor between categories which sends a class of arrows
\mathsf{W}_{\mathsf{C}}
in
\mathsf{C}
to a class of arrows
\mathsf{W}_{\mathsf{D}}
in
\mathsf{D}
, we still denote

\mathsf{F}:\mathsf{C}\penalty\ [\mathsf{W}^{-1}_{\mathsf{C}}]\longrightarrow\mathsf{D}\penalty\ [\mathsf{W}^{-1}_{\mathsf{D}}]

the induced functor at the
\infty
-categorical level. However, we will add
\mathbb{L}\mathsf{F}
and
\mathbb{R}\mathsf{F}
to the left (resp. right) derived functors of
\mathsf{F}
when it is a left (resp. right) Quillen functor which does not preserve weak equivalences in general. If
\mathsf{C}
is a 1-category that we consider as an
\infty
-category via the nerve functor
\mathcal{N}
, we will still denote it by
\mathsf{C}
instead of
\mathcal{N}(\mathsf{C})
.

•

Our base
\infty
-category will be the category of chain complexes over
\Bbbk
localized at the class of all quasi-isomorphisms
\mathsf{Ch}(\Bbbk)\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]
, which a symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category. We will denote this
\infty
-category directly by
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
.

•

In general, an enriched
\infty
-operad in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
will be denoted by
\mathscr{P}
, and an enriched
\infty
-cooperad by
\mathscr{C}
. Given any enriched
\infty
-operad
\mathscr{P}
, we denote the
\infty
-category of
\mathscr{P}
-algebras in the base
\infty
-category
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
by
\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))
, and the
\infty
-category of
\mathscr{P}
-coalgebras in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
by
\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))
. Similarly for algebras and coalgebras over an enriched
\infty
-cooperad.

1.
Recollections on algebras and coalgebras

The goal of this section is to introduce both the point-set and the
\infty
-categorical framework that we are going to use in the rest of this paper.

On the point-set side, we recall the notions of operads and cooperads, as well as the categories of algebras and coalgebras that one can define over them. Then we explain the operadic bar-cobar adjunction between augmented dg operads and conilpotent dg cooperads and how one obtains induced bar-cobar adjunctions between their respective categories of algebras and coalgebras. Finally, we also explain the different model structures that each of these categories can be endowed with.

On the
\infty
-categorical side, we use the formalism of symmetric sequences developed in
[
14
, Section 4.1.2]
, where one can define (enriched)
\infty
-operads (resp. (enriched)
\infty
-cooperads) as monoids (resp. comonoids) in the
\infty
-category of symmetric sequences. See also
[
31
,
30
]
for a different approach to this formalism. We first recall the definitions of algebras over
\infty
-operads and (divided powers, conilpotent) coalgebras over
\infty
-cooperads. Then, we recall the main points of the definition of coalgebras over an operad introduced in
[
48
]
. And finally, we introduce the notion of an algebra over a
\infty
-cooperad in the
\infty
-categorical context, in a spirit similar to
[
27
]
. See also
[
53
]
for more details on algebras over cooperads.

1.1.
Point-set preliminaries

As in
[
48
, Section 1]
, we consider the monoidal category
\mathsf{sSeq}(\mathsf{Ch}(\Bbbk))
of dg symmetric sequences, whose objects are collections
\{M(n)\}_{n\geq 0}
of chain complexes endowed with an action of
\mathbb{S}_{n}
for all
n\geq 0
, with monoidal structure given by the
composition product
.

A dg operad is a monoid object
\mathcal{P}
in
\mathsf{sSeq}(\mathsf{Ch}(\Bbbk))
. Dually, a cooperad is a comonoid object in
\mathsf{sSeq}(\mathsf{Ch}(\Bbbk))
. In
[
48
, Section 1]
, we defined algebras and coalgebras over a dg operad
\mathcal{P}
in terms of the
endomorphism operad
and
coendomorphism operad
associated with a dg vector space. We now want to firstly give an equivalent, monadic, definition of the category of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras. The monadic definition naturally dualizes instead to a comonadic definition of a
coalgebra over a cooperad
.

1.1.1.
Algebras over operads and coalgebras over cooperads.

To any dg symmetric sequence one can associate an endofunctor in the category of chain complexes via the
Schur realization functor
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{S}(-):\mathsf{sSeq}(\mathsf{Ch}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{End}(\mathsf{Ch}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{S}(M)\coloneqq\displaystyle\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}M(n)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}(-)^{\otimes n}\penalty\ .}

The Schur realization functor
\mathrm{S}(-)
is strong monoidal. Thus, for any dg operad
\mathcal{P}
its Schur functor
\mathrm{S}(\mathcal{P})
is a monad in chain complexes, and for any dg cooperad
\mathcal{C}
its Schur functor
\mathrm{S}(\mathcal{C})
is a comonad in chain complexes.

Definition 1.1

(dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad. A dg
\mathcal{P}
-
algebra
is an algebra over the monad
\mathrm{S}(\mathcal{P})
.

Definition 1.2

(dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a dg cooperad. A dg
\mathcal{C}
-
coalgebra

W
is a coalgebra over the comonad
\mathrm{S}(\mathcal{C})
.

Remark 1.3

(About coalgebras over cooperads)
.

The structural map of a dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
W

\Delta_{W}:W\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{C}(n)\otimes_{\mathbb S_{n}}W^{\otimes n}

lands in the direct sum. Therefore any element
w
in
W
can only be decomposed into a
finite sum
. This implies that dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras always satisfy a
conilpotency
condition. Furthermore, the fact that
\Delta_{W}
lands in the
coinvariants
on the right-hand side implies that
divided power operations
will appear in this type of structure. Therefore the definition of a dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra encodes divided powers conilpotent types of coalgebraic structures.

1.1.2.
Algebras over a cooperad

There is a
dual Schur realization functor
which is given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}:\mathsf{sSeq}(\mathsf{Ch}(\Bbbk))^{\mathsf{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{End}(\mathsf{Ch}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(M)\coloneqq\displaystyle\prod_{n\geq 0}[M(n),(-)^{\otimes n}]^{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\penalty\ .}

The functor
\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(-)
is lax monoidal by
[
27
, Corollary 3.4]
: therefore any dg cooperad induces a monad on the category of chain complexes.

Definition 1.4

(dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra)
.

A
dg

\mathcal{C}
-algebra

B
is an algebra
(B,\gamma_{B},d_{B})
over the monad
\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{C})
.

Remark 1.5

(About algebras over cooperads, and absolute algebras)
.

The structural data of a dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra is a morphism

\gamma_{B}:\prod_{n\geq 0}[\mathcal{C}(n),B^{\otimes n}]^{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\longrightarrow B\penalty\ .

This morphism associates to any formal power series of operations in
\mathcal{C}
labelled by elements of
B
a well-defined image in
B
, without supposing an underlying topology on
B
. In
[
53
]
, the second named author introduced the terminology of
absolute algebras
for what is here called an algebra over a cooperad. For example, an “absolute Lie algebra” is in the present terminology simply an algebra over the cooperad
\mathcal{L}\mathit{ie}^{*}
, the linear dual of
\mathcal{L}\mathit{ie}
. One defines e.g. “absolute associative algebras” and “absolute commutative algebras” similarly. Notice that since the above definition uses
invariants
, these operations have
divided power operations
by default as well. We refer to
[
53
]
for more on this type of structure.

1.1.3.
The canonical filtration on algebras over cooperads.

The notion of an algebra over a cooperad admits a further description in the case where the cooperad is
conilpotent
. Recall that, for any dg cooperad, one can define the subcooperad
{R}_{p}\mathcal{C}
of operations in
\mathcal{C}
which admit at most
p
-fold iterated non-trivial partial decompositions, and that
\mathcal{C}
is said to be
conilpotent
if

\colim_{p}{R}_{p}\mathcal{C}\cong\mathcal{C}\penalty\ .

See
[
52
, Section 1.2]
for this particular definition of conilpotency, which is in fact equivalent to the one given in
[
42
, Chapter 5.8]
.

Definition 1.6

(Canonical filtration on a dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent dg cooperad and let
B
be a dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra. The
canonical filtration
of
B
is the decreasing filtration given by

{W}^{p}B\coloneqq\mathrm{Im}\left(\gamma_{B}\circ\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\pi_{p})(\mathrm{id}_{B}):\widehat{\mathscr{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{C}/{R}_{p}\mathcal{C})(B)\longrightarrow B\right)

where
{R}_{p}\mathcal{C}
denotes the
p
-th term of the coradical filtration, for all
p\geq 0\penalty\ .
Notice that we have

B={W}^{0}B\supseteq{W}^{1}B\supseteq{W}^{2}B\supseteq\cdots\supseteq\cdots.

Each step
{W}^{p}B
of the canonical filtration of a dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
B
is an ideal, meaning the quotient
B/{W}^{k}B
has a canonical dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra structure induced by the original dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra structure of
B
.

Definition 1.7

(Completion of a dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra)
.

Let
B
be a dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra. Its
completion
is given by the limit

\widehat{B}\coloneqq\lim_{p}B/{W}^{p}B\penalty\ ,

taken in the category of dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras.

Any dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
B
comes equipped with a canonical morphism of dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras

\varphi_{B}:B\longrightarrow\widehat{B}

This morphism is always an epimorphism, see
[
27
, Proposition 4.24]
. We define
complete
dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras as the full subcategory of dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras for which this map is an isomorphism. It is a reflective subcategory of dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras, where the reflector is given by the completion functor. Notice that any free dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra is in particular complete.

1.1.4.
Coalgebras over an operad

For any dg operad
\mathcal{P}
, its dual Schur functor
\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P})
fails to be a comonad since the dual Schur functor is only lax monoidal. However, this lax structure endows this endofunctor with a lax comonad structure over which one can define coalgebras.

Definition 1.8

(dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra)
.

A
dg

\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra

C
is a chain complex
(C,d_{C})
endowed with a structural map

\Delta_{C}:C\longrightarrow\displaystyle\prod_{n\geq 0}[\mathcal{P}(n),C^{\otimes n}]^{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\penalty\ ,

such that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P})(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P})\circ\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P})(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P})(C)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P})\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathrm{id})(\Delta_{C})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}}(C)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\gamma)(\mathrm{id})}

The data of a dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra
C
is equivalent to the data of a morphism of dg operads
\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathrm{coEnd}_{C}
, where
\mathrm{coEnd}_{C}
denotes the coendomorphism operad of
C
, given by the dg
\mathbb{S}_{n}
-module

\mathrm{coEnd}_{C}(n)\coloneqq[C,C^{\otimes n}]\penalty\ ,

for all
n\geq 0
, where the operad structure is given by the usual composition of maps.

Remark 1.9

(About coalgebras over operads)
.

The structure morphism of a dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra
C
is equivalent to a family of maps

\Delta_{C}^{n}:C\longrightarrow[\mathcal{P}(n),C^{\otimes n}]^{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\penalty\ ,

for all
n\geq 0
. These maps assign to every element
c
in
C
its decompositions with respect to all the operations in
\mathcal{P}(n)
, which lie in
C^{\otimes n}
. This definition encodes the usual types of coalgebraic structures one encounters. For instance, if
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{C}om
is the commutative operad, dg
\mathcal{C}om
-coalgebras are precisely non-unital dg commutative coalgebras. If
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{A}ss
is the associative operad, then dg
\mathcal{A}ss
-coalgebras are non-unital dg associative coalgebras. In both cases, this gives the categories of all coalgebras without imposing a conilpotency condition.

Theorem 1.10

(
[
2
, Theorem 2.7.11]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad. The category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras is comonadic. There exists a comonad
(\mathrm{L}(\mathcal{P}),\omega,\zeta)
in the category of chain complexes such that the category of
\mathrm{L}(\mathcal{P})
-coalgebras is equivalent to the category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras.

In particular, this entails the existence of a cofree dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra. While in the general setting of
[
2
]
, the construction of the comonad
\mathrm{L}(\mathcal{P})
is given by an infinite recursion, the construction of
\mathrm{L}(\mathcal{P})
in the category of chain complexes over a field
\Bbbk
stops at the first step. It is thus given by the following pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{L}(\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P})\circ\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P}\circ\mathcal{P})\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{\mathcal{P},\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\gamma)}

1.1.5.
Operadic Koszul duality

Let us now recall the bar-cobar adjunction that exists between dg operads and conilpotent dg cooperads.

Definition 1.11

(Reduced dg operad)
.

A dg operad
\mathcal{P}
is
reduced
if the unit morphism
I\to\mathcal{P}
is an isomorphism in arities 0 and 1. Similarly, a cooperad
\mathcal{C}
is reduced if its counit
\mathcal{C}\to I
is an isomorphism in arity 0 and 1.

From now on, we shall always quietly
assume that all dg operads and dg cooperads are reduced
. Under this hypothesis, there is an adjunction, going back to Ginzburg–Kapranov
[
25
]
and Getzler–Jones
[
24
]

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Operads}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Cooperads},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between dg operads and dg cooperads. Notice that reduced dg cooperads are automatically conilpotent.

A
twisting morphism

\alpha:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
is a graded morphism of degree
-1
which satisfies the equation

\alpha\circ d_{\mathcal{C}}-d_{\mathcal{P}}\circ\alpha+\alpha\star\alpha=0\penalty\ ,

where
\alpha\star\alpha
is given by the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\circ_{(1)}\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\circ_{(1)}\mathcal{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{(1)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha\penalty\ \circ_{(1)}\penalty\ \alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{(1)}}

Here
\Delta_{(1)}
and
\gamma_{(1)}
refer to the infinitesimal decomposition of
\mathcal{C}
and to the infinitesimal composition of
\mathcal{P}
, respectively. The equation above corresponds to the Maurer-Cartan equation in the convolution pre-Lie algebra constructed from
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{P}
. The bar-cobar adjunction represents and corepresents twisting morphisms between dg cooperads and dg operads:

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Operads}}(\Omega\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\cong\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{P})\cong\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathsf{Cooperads}}(\mathcal{C},\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P})\penalty\ .

We refer to
[
42
, Chapter 6]
for more details.

1.1.6.
Classical bar-cobar adjunction induced by a twisting morphism

Any twisting morphism
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
induces a
bar-cobar adjunction
relative to
\alpha

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between the category of dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras and the category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras. For any dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A
, the bar construction
\mathrm{B}_{\alpha}A
is given by taking the cofree
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra on
A
and building a differential using the algebra structure on
A
and the twisting morphism
\alpha
. Similarly, for any dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra
W
, the cobar construction
\Omega_{\alpha}W
is given by taking the free
\mathcal{P}
-algebra on
W
and building a differential using the coalgebra structure on
W
and the twisting morphism
\alpha
. We refer to
[
42
, Chapter 11]
for more details on these topics.

1.1.7.
Complete bar-cobar adjunction induced by a twisting morphism

Any twisting morphism
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
between a conilpotent dg cooperad
\mathcal{C}
and a dg operad
\mathcal{P}
induces a second bar-cobar adjunction, which we call the
complete bar-cobar adjunction
relative to
\alpha

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between the category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras and the category of complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras. Like in the previous case, this adjunction is given by considering free and cofree objects, and endowing them with the appropriate differential. See
[
27
]
or
[
28
]
for more details.

1.1.8.
Admissible and coadmissible operads

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad. It is natural to consider dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras and dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras up to quasi-isomorphisms. We say that
\mathcal{P}
is
admissible
if the projective model structure on chain complexes over
\Bbbk
can be transferred along the free-forgetful adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Ch}(\Bbbk)\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{S}(\mathcal{P})(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

thus endowing the category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras with a model structure where weak equivalences are given by quasi-isomorphisms and fibrations are given by degree-wise surjections. Any dg operad over a field of characteristic zero is admissible
[
37
]
.

Dually, we say that
\mathcal{P}
is
coadmissible
if the injective model structure on chain complexes over
\Bbbk
can be transferred along the cofree-forgetful adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Ch}(\Bbbk)\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{L}(\mathcal{P})(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

thus endowing the category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras with a model structure where weak equivalences are given by quasi-isomorphisms and cofibrations are given by degree-wise injections. It is not true, even over a field of characteristic zero, that any dg operad is coadmissible, see
[
27
, Proposition 8.10]
for a counter-example. However, cofibrant dg operads (and therefore dg operads of the form
\Omega\mathcal{C}
) are always coadmissible by standard arguments as in
[
11
]
. See
[
27
,
28
]
for more details as well.

1.1.9.
Model structures transferred along the classical bar-cobar adjunction

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad (which is always admissible in our context). The model structure transferred from chain complexes over
\Bbbk
presents the
\infty
-category of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras up to quasi-isomorphisms
[
39
,
31
]
. It is sometimes convenient to transfer this model structure along a bar-cobar adjunction to a suitable category of coalgebras over a cooperad. In the good cases, when the twisting morphism
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
is said to be
Koszul
, one obtains a Quillen equivalence between dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras and dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras with the transferred structure. We refer to
[
57
,
20
]
for the following results in characteristic zero, and to
[
28
]
to a generalization over a positive characteristic field.

Theorem 1.12
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent dg cooperad. There exists a combinatorial model category structure on the category of dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras, sometimes called the canonical model structure, given by the following classes of maps:

(1)

the class of weak equivalences is given by morphisms
f
such that
\Omega_{\alpha}(f)
is a quasi-isomorphism,

(2)

the class of cofibrations is given by degree-wise injections,

(3)

the class of fibrations is given by morphisms with the right lifting property with respect to acyclic cofibrations.

Furthermore, if
\alpha:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
is Koszul in the sense of
[
42
, Chapter 6]
, the bar-cobar adjunction relative to
\alpha
is a Quillen equivalence.

Example 1.13
.

The universal twisting morphisms
\iota:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\Omega\mathcal{C}
and
\pi:\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
(induced by the unit and the counit of the operadic bar-cobar adjunction) are both Koszul twisting morphisms.

Remark 1.14
.

Over a positive characteristic field, one needs to add the hypothesis that the conilpotent dg cooperad
\mathcal{C}
is furthermore
quasi-planar
in the sense of
[
28
]
, and one has to restrict to the twisting morphism
\iota:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\Omega\mathcal{C}
. However, if our goal is to understand the
\infty
-category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras on a
\mathbb{S}
-cofibrant operad
\mathcal{P}
, then we can always replace
\mathcal{P}
with a dg operad of the form
\Omega\mathcal{C}
, where
\mathcal{C}
is a quasi-planar conilpotent dg cooperad, without changing the underlying
\infty
-category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras. A canonical choice is given by
\mathcal{C}=\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{E})
, which is indeed quasi-planar for any dg operad
\mathcal{P}
, and
\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{E})
is always quasi-isomorphic to
\mathcal{P}
.

Notice that in Theorem
1.12
, the class of weak equivalences is
strictly
included in the class of quasi-isomorphisms of dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras, unless the twisting morphism is the terminal one given by
\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{I}
. Localizing at this class of weak equivalences does not present an intrinsic
\infty
-category of coalgebras in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
; on the contrary, since the bar-cobar adjunction is (in the good cases) a Quillen equivalence, it provides us with another presentation of the underlying
\infty
-category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras. However, as shown in
[
20
]
, the model category of dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras admits a left Bousfield localization with respect to quasi-isomorphisms.

Proposition 1.15
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent dg cooperad. There exists a combinatorial model structure on dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras transferred from chain complexes, determined by the following classes of morphisms

(1)

the class of cofibrations is given by degree-wise injections,

(2)

the class of weak equivalences is given by quasi-isomorphisms,

(3)

the class of fibrations is determined by the right-lifting property against all acyclic cofibrations.

Moreover, this is a left Bousfield localisation of the canonical model structure transferred from dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-algebras (which coincides with the model structure transferred along any Koszul twisting morphism). This means that the identity functor of dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras, where at the source they are endowed with the canonical model structure, and at the target with the quasi-isomorphisms model structure, is a left Quillen functor.

Remark 1.16
.

Over a positive characteristic field, one needs again to add the extra hypothesis that
\mathcal{C}
is
quasi-planar
. See
[
28
, Section 5.6]
for more details.

1.1.10.
Model structures transferred along the complete bar-cobar adjunction

As mentioned before, not all operads are coadmissible, even over a field of characteristic zero. Furthermore, even when they are, they need not present their
\infty
-categorical counterparts, as for instance shown in
[
47
]
. Nevertheless, one can solve these problems by working with cofibrant dg operads, as they do present their
\infty
-categorical counterparts, see
[
48
]
. In fact, without any loss of generality, we may assume that the cofibrant dg operad is of the form
\Omega\mathcal{C}
, where
\mathcal{C}
is a conilpotent dg cooperad. In this context, dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras up to quasi-isomorphisms present the correct
\infty
-category. Furthermore, they admit a transferred model structure from chain complexes. Indeed, since
\Omega\mathcal{C}
is cofibrant it is in particular coadmissible. It is sometimes convenient to transfer this model structure along the complete bar-cobar adjunction in order to obtain a Quillen equivalent model structure on dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras
[
27
]
.

Theorem 1.17
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent dg cooperad. There exists a combinatorial model category structure on the category of complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras given by the following classes of maps:

(1)

the class of weak equivalences is given by morphisms
f
such that
\widehat{B}_{\iota}(f)
is a quasi-isomorphism,

(2)

the class of fibrations is given by degree-wise surjections,

(3)

the class of cofibrations is given by morphisms with the left-lifting property with respect to acyclic fibrations.

Furthermore, when endowed with this model structure, the complete bar-cobar adjunction relative to
\iota:\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\Omega\mathcal{C}
becomes a Quillen equivalence.

Remark 1.18
.

This result admits a generalization for a quasi-planar conilpotent dg cooperad over a field of positive characteristic, see
[
28
, Section 6 and 7]
.

Again, notice that the class of weak equivalences is
strictly
included in the class of quasi-isomorphisms of complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras. Localizing at this class of weak equivalences does not present an
\infty
-category of algebras in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
. On the contrary, since the complete bar-cobar adjunction is a Quillen equivalence, it gives another presentation of the underlying
\infty
-category of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. However, complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras admit a right Bousfield localization with respect to quasi-isomorphisms.

Proposition 1.19
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent dg cooperad. There exists a combinatorial model structure on qp-complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras transferred from chain complexes, determined by the following classes of morphisms

(1)

the class of fibrations is given by degree-wise surjections,

(2)

the class of weak equivalences is given by quasi-isomorphisms,

(3)

the class of cofibrations is determined by the left-lifting property against all acyclic fibrations.

Moreover, this is a right Bousfield localisation of the canonical model structure transferred from dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras. That is, the identity functor of dg
\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras, where at the source they are endowed with the canonical model structure, and at the target with the quasi-isomorphisms model structure, is a right Quillen functor.

Remark 1.20
.

See
[
28
, Section 7.5]
for the general statement involving the quasi-planar hypothesis, which reduces to the above proposition over a characteristic zero field.

1.2.
\infty
-categorical preliminaries

In this section, we work with the formalism of
\infty
-categorical symmetric sequences developed in
[
14
, Section 4.1.2]
. See also
[
54
, Section 5.2.4]
for a detailed exposition of these ideas. We use this formalism to define both algebras and coalgebras over enriched
\infty
-(co)operads. Note that by the recent work of
[
3
]
, this formalism of enriched
\infty
-operads is equivalent to the one developped in
[
19
]
.

1.2.1.
Symmetric sequences, operads and cooperads.

For simplicity, we work over the base symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category of chain complexes over
\Bbbk
up to quasi-isomorphisms
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
, although these definitions make sense in a more general context of a rigidly compactly generated symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
[
48
, Section 6]
.

Let
\mathsf{Fin}^{\simeq}
denote the 1-category of finite sets and bijections. We define the
\infty
-category of
symmetric sequences
in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
as the
\infty
-category of functors from
\mathsf{Fin}^{\simeq}
to
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
:

\mathbf{sSeq}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\coloneqq\mathbf{Fun}(\mathsf{Fin}^{\simeq},\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ .

For
M
in
\mathbf{sSeq}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))
, we denote by
M(n)
the evaluation of
M
at the set
\underline{n}=\{1,\cdots,n\}
. The
\infty
-category of symmetric sequences in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
admits a monoidal structure given by the composition product
\circledcirc
, which for two symmetric sequences
M
and
N
is given pointwise by

M\circledcirc N(n)\simeq\bigoplus_{k\geq 0}\left(\bigoplus_{\underline{n}=\sqcup_{i=1}^{k}S_{i}}M(k)\otimes N(S_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes N(S_{k})\right)_{h\mathbb{S}_{k}}

as computed in
[
54
, Appendix A.1.]
. Using this composition product, one can define operads as monoids in a monoidal
\infty
-category.

Definition 1.21

(
\infty
-operad)
.

An
\infty
-operad
is a monoid object in the
\infty
-category of symmetric sequences with respect to the composition product.

Definition 1.22

(augmented
\infty
-operad)
.

An
augmented

\infty
-operad
is an
\infty
-operad
\mathscr{P}
together with a map of
\infty
-operads
\epsilon:\mathscr{P}\longrightarrow\mathbb{1}
.

Definition 1.23

(
\infty
-cooperad)
.

An
\infty
-cooperad
is a comonoid object in the
\infty
-category of symmetric sequences.

Definition 1.24

(coaugmented
\infty
-cooperad)
.

A
coaugmented
\infty
-cooperad
is an
\infty
-cooperad
\mathscr{C}
with a map
\mu:\mathbb{1}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}
of
\infty
-cooperads.

1.2.2.
Lurie’s bar-cobar adjunction between
\infty
-operads and
\infty
-cooperads

In
[
43
]
, Lurie constructs a general bar-cobar adjunction between augmented monoids and coaugmented comonoids over a pointed monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathbf{C}
admitting geometric realizations of simplicial objects and totalizations of cosimplicial objects. See
[
43
, Section 4.3]
or Section
7

for more details. By applying this general construction to
\mathbf{C}=\mathbf{sSeq}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))
, we get the following adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\infty\mbox{-}\mathbf{Op}^{\mathbf{aug}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\infty\mbox{-}\mathbf{Coop}^{\mathbf{coaug}}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{bar}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{cobar}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

which can be further restricted to an adjunction between reduced
\infty
-operads and reduced
\infty
-cooperads. The bar construction of an
\infty
-operad
\mathscr{P}
is computed as the geometric realization of the simplicial object

\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P})\coloneqq\left|\penalty\ \mathbf{bar}(\mathbb{1},\mathscr{P},\mathbb{1})_{\bullet}\penalty\ \right|=\left|\hbox to187.14pt{\vbox to31.03pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 93.56934pt\lower-15.51646pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
