Furthermore, this adjunction becomes an equivalence of
\infty
-categories between reduced
\infty
-operads and reduced
\infty
-cooperads by
[
36
, Theorem 3.4]
.

Remark 1.25
.

The point-set operadic bar construction of Paragraph
1.1.5
is a model for the
\infty
-categorical bar construction of the underlying
\infty
-operad. Indeed, the two constructions can be compared using the same arguments as in
[
14
, Section 5.4.2]
.

1.2.3.
Schur functors associated to enriched
\infty
-(co)operads and (co)algebras

As in the point-set setting, there is a functor

\mathbf{S}(-):\mathbf{sSeq}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\longrightarrow\mathbf{End}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ ,

from the
\infty
-category of symmetric sequences in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
to the
\infty
-category of endofunctors in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
. It associates to any symmetric sequence
M
an endofunctor
\mathbf{S}(M)
, given by the formula

\mathbf{S}(M)=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\left(M(n)\otimes(-)^{\otimes n}\right)_{h\mathbb{S}_{n}}\penalty\ .

The functor
\mathbf{S}(-)
is symmetric monoidal, and therefore sends (enriched)
\infty
-operads to monads and (enriched)
\infty
-cooperads to comonads.

Definition 1.26

(
\mathscr{P}
-algebras)
.

A
\mathscr{P}
-algebra
is an algebra over the monad
\mathbf{S}(\mathscr{P})
.

Remark 1.27
.

A
\mathscr{P}
-algebra is an object
A
in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
equipped with a family of structural maps

\gamma_{n}^{A}:\left(\mathscr{P}(n)\otimes A^{\otimes n}\right)_{h\mathbb{S}_{n}}\longrightarrow A

for all
n\geq 1
which satisfy associativity and unitality conditions up to higher coherences.

Remark 1.28

(Point-set models for
\mathscr{P}
-algebras)
.

Any
\infty
-operad enriched in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
admits a point-set model, given by some dg operad in chain complexes over
\Bbbk
. One sees this by comparing their respective
\infty
-categories of algebras in the following way: let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad, projective as a dg symmetric sequence. Then there is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\simeq\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))

between dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras up to quasi-isomorphism and algebras in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
over the induced enriched
\infty
-operad. This follows from the Barr–Beck–Lurie theorem, since the point-set Schur functor presents the
\infty
-categorical Schur functor when the underlying dg symmetric sequence of
\mathcal{P}
is projective. We refer to
[
39
,
46
,
31
]
for similar statements.

Definition 1.29

(Divided powers conilpotent
\mathscr{C}
-coalgebras)
.

A
divided powers conilpotent
\mathscr{C}
-coalgebra

W
is a coalgebra over the comonad
\mathbf{S}(\mathscr{C})
.

Remark 1.30
.

A divided powers conilpotent
\mathscr{C}
-coalgebra is an object
W
in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
equipped with a structural map

\Delta_{W}:W\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\left(\mathscr{C}(n)\otimes W^{\otimes n}\right)_{h\mathbb{S}_{n}}\penalty\ ,

which again, satisfies some associativity condition up to higher coherences. Since
\Delta_{W}
lands in an infinite coproduct instead of an infinite product, this implies that decomposition has to have some finiteness condition, which explains the adjective conilpotent. Furthermore, this structural map lands on the homotopy orbits, hence this decomposition operation has a divided powers structure as well.

Remark 1.31
.

In
[
36
]
, Heuts proposed a definition of divided powers
\mathscr{C}
-coalgebras for a cooperad
\mathscr{C}
which, roughly, replaces the infinite coproduct in the above definition with an infinite product, thus removing the conilpotency constraint on the decomposition map.

1.2.4.
Dual Schur functor and algebras over an enriched
\infty
-cooperad

There is another Schur functor associated to any symmetric sequence, which we call the
dual Schur functor
. Associating to a symmetric sequence
M
its dual Schur functor defines a functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathbf{S}}^{c}(-):\mathbf{sSeq}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))^{\mathsf{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{End}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\prod_{n\geq 0}\left[M(n),(-)^{\otimes n}\right]^{h\mathbb{S}_{n}}}

from the
\infty
-category of symmetric sequences in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
to the
\infty
-category of endofunctors of
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
. Here
[-,-]
denotes the self-enrichment of
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
, adjoint to the tensor product.

Lemma 1.32
.

The functor
\widehat{\mathbf{S}}^{c}(-)
is lax monoidal.

Proof.

We adapt the proof given in
[
41
, Proposition 5.1]
to the
\infty
-categorical setting.
∎

Remark 1.33
.

The 1-categorical complete Schur functor
\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(-)
is lax monoidal. The natural map

\mathsf{Alg}(\mathsf{End}(\mathsf{Ch}(\Bbbk)))[\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\longrightarrow\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbb{E}_{1}}(\mathbf{End}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)))

sends it to
\widehat{\mathbf{S}}^{c}(-)
, hence its
\infty
-categorical counterpart is also lax monoidal, providing another proof of
Lemma

1.32
.

Lemma

1.32
implies that an (enriched)
\infty
-cooperad
\mathscr{C}
in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
is sent to a monad in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
. Therefore, it also makes sense to define algebras over an
\infty
-cooperad.

Definition 1.34

(
\mathscr{C}
-algebra)
.

A
\mathscr{C}
-algebra is an algebra over the monad
\widehat{\mathbf{S}}^{c}(\mathscr{C})
.

Remark 1.35
.

A
\mathscr{C}
-algebra is an object
B
in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
equipped with a structural map

\gamma^{B}:\prod_{n\geq 0}\left[\mathscr{C}(n),B^{\otimes n}\right]^{h\mathbb{S}_{n}}\longrightarrow B

which satisfies associativity and unitality conditions up to higher coherences. This structure is the analogue of the
absolute algebra
structures studied in
[
53
]
at the homotopical level.

1.2.5.
Coalgebras over operads

We briefly recall the definition of the
\infty
-category of coalgebras over an (enriched)
\infty
-operad given in
[
48
, Section 2]
.

The main obstruction to defining
\mathscr{P}
-coalgebras is that
\widehat{\mathbf{S}}^{c}(-)
is only lax monoidal, therefore the image of an
\infty
-operad is not a priori a comonad. Following the ideas of
[
36
, Appendix A]
, we extended this dual Schur functor to the
\infty
-category of pro-objects
\mathbf{pro}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))
, such that the assignment

\widehat{\mathbf{S}}^{c}_{\mathrm{pro}}(-):\mathbf{sSeq}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))^{\mathsf{op}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{End}(\mathbf{pro}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)))

is now a strong monoidal functor. Therefore it makes sense to define
\mathscr{P}
-coalgebras in
\mathbf{pro}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))
as coalgebras over this comonad.

Finally, if we denote by
c:\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)\longrightarrow\mathbf{pro}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))
the canonical fully faithful inclusion given by sending objects in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
to their constant pro-object, one can pullback along this inclusion to obtain a well-behaved definition of
\mathscr{P}
-coalgebras in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)

Definition 1.36

(
\mathscr{P}
-coalgebra)
.

The
\infty
-category of
\mathscr{P}
-coalgebras
as the following pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\widehat{\mathbf{S}}^{c}_{\mathrm{pro}}(\mathscr{P})}(\mathbf{pro}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{pro}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

in the
\infty
-category of
\infty
-categories.

The
\infty
-category of
\mathscr{P}
-coalgebras is presentable and comonadic over the base
\infty
-category
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
. When
\mathscr{P}
is the linearization of an
\infty
-operad enriched in spaces, this definition recovers the definitions of coalgebras over an
\infty
-operad considered in
[
43
, Chapter 2]
, given by the opposite category of
\mathscr{P}
-algebras in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)^{\mathbf{op}}
. See
[
48
, Theorem B]
for more details.

Moreover, let us recall the main theorem of
[
48
]
, which compares the
\infty
-category of point-set coalgebras over a cofibrant dg operad localized at quasi-isomorphisms with coalgebras (in the sense above) over the underlying enriched
\infty
-operad of the dg operad.

Theorem 1.37

(
[
48
, Main theorem]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a cofibrant dg operad. There is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\simeq\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))

between dg
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras up to quasi-isomorphism and coalgebras in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
over the induced enriched
\infty
-operad.

In particular, this means that the point-set description of the cofree
\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra functor given in
[
2
]
also describes the
\infty
-categorical cofree construction. For more details about this definition and its properties, we refer to
[
48
]
.

2.
The point-set inclusion-restriction square

In this section, we construct a commutative square of Quillen adjunctions which we call the inclusion-restriction square. It intertwines two natural restriction and inclusion functors with the bar-cobar and the complete bar-cobar adjunctions. We furthermore show that this particular 1-categorical square of Quillen adjunctions induces an adjunction at the
\infty
-categorical level. The left and the right
\infty
-adjoints are both obtained by composing left and right Quillen functors, which is a somewhat surprising phenomenon.

Notation 2.1
.

For the rest of this paper,
\mathcal{P}
will always denote a dg operad and
\mathcal{C}
will always denote a conilpotent dg cooperad.

Operads and cooperads throughout this paper will be subject to the following set of assumptions.

Assumption 1
.

(1)

Their underlying dg symmetric sequences are reduced, meaning they are trivial in arity
0
and
\Bbbk
in arity
1
.

(2)

Either the ground field
\Bbbk
is of characteristic zero, or the dg operads and conilpotent dg cooperads are non-symmetric.

(3)

For all
n\geq 0
, the dg
\mathbb{S}_{n}
-module of arity
n
operations is a degree-wise finite dimensional and bounded complex. Hence it is of finite total dimension over
\Bbbk
.

Let us make some remarks on these assumptions.

•

It follows from (1) that any dg operad is augmented, and any dg cooperad is both conilpotent and coaugmented.

•

It follows from (3) that the linear dual of a dg operad is a dg cooperad, and vice versa.

•

These assumptions are stable under the operadic bar-cobar adjunction.

•

Under assumption (2), most examples of interest satisfy (3). If (2) fails, then (3) is unnatural: for example, any
\mathbb E_{\infty}
operad in positive characteristic is infinite-dimensional in each arity
>1
. In that setting (cf.
[
28
, Section 2]
), one would need to relax assumption (3) so that the dg
\mathbb{S}_{n}
-module of arity
n
operations is degree-wise finite dimensional and only bounded-below, in order to include examples like the quasi-planar dg cooperads of the form
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{E})
, where
\mathcal{E}
is the Barratt–Eccles operad
[
10
]
.

Remark 2.2

(Starting with an operad or a cooperad.)
.

For the rest of this section, we are going to start with dg operad
\mathcal{P}
, and we will use many times the conilpotent dg cooperad
\mathcal{P}^{*}
given by its linear dual. This is a matter of convention,
the same constructions can be done
starting with a a conilpotent dg cooperad
\mathcal{C}
and its linear dual dg cooperad
\mathcal{C}^{*}
.

2.1.
The restriction functor and its left adjoint

The goal of this subsection is to recall some of the constructions of
[
53
, Section 3]
. In particular, we explain the adjunction between dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebras and dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras given by restricting the absolute structure to finite sums, as well as some of its main properties.

Proposition 2.3
.

There is an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c\mathrm{Abs}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebras and dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras, where the right adjoint
\mathrm{Res}
is given by restricting the structure to finite sums of operations.

Proof.

There is a natural inclusion of monads

\iota_{\mathcal{P}}:\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{P}(n)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}(-)^{\otimes n}\hookrightarrow\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}(\mathcal{P}^{*}(n),(-)^{\otimes n})\penalty\ ,

given by the composition of the canonical inclusion
V^{*}\otimes W\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Hom}(V,W)
, the norm map from coinvariants to invariants and the inclusion of the direct sum into the direct product. Pulling the structure map of a dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebra along
\iota
induces the restriction functor
\mathrm{Res}
from dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebras to dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras. It is an accessible functor which preserves all limits between two presentable categories, hence it admits a left adjoint
c\mathrm{Abs}
. By post-composing this adjoint with the completion functor from dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebras to complete dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebras, we obtain the functor
c\mathrm{Abs}
above and the desired adjunction.
∎

Theorem 2.4

(
[
53
, Theorem 3.16]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumption
1
. The restriction functor

\mathrm{Res}:\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}

is fully faithful. Therefore dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras are a reflective subcategory of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras.

Remark 2.5
.

Let us consider the case of commutative algebras, where
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{C}om
. It is not true that complete absolute dg commutative algebras are equivalent to
I
-adically complete dg commutative algebras. This is because the
I
-adic completion is not in general idempotent, unless one restricts to the noetherian case. By contrast, complete absolute dg commutative algebras can always be described as algebras over an idempotent monad on the category of dg commutative algebras. See
[
53
, Proposition 3.24]
and the subsequent discussion for more details.

2.2.
The inclusion functor and its right adjoint

We now compare dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras with dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras, which encode, respectively, the conilpotent and non-necessarily conilpotent versions of the same coalgebra structures.

Proposition 2.6
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumption
1
. There is an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sub}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Inc}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras and dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras, where the left adjoint
\mathrm{Inc}
is given by viewing a dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra as a dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebra.

Proof.

As in the proof of Proposition
2.3
, there is a natural inclusion

\iota_{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}:\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}(n)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}(-)^{\otimes n}\hookrightarrow\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}(\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}(n),(-)^{\otimes n})\penalty\ ,

since
(\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*})^{*}
is isomorphic to
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
. The latter fact follows from the dualizability hypothesis in Assumption
1
. This natural transformation of functors induces an inclusion of comonads

\iota_{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}:\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}(n)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}(-)^{\otimes n}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{L}(\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*})(-)\penalty\ ,

since it can be checked to be compatible with the pullback square that defines the cofree functor
\mathrm{L}(\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*})(-)
in Theorem
1.10
. Pushing forward the structural morphism of a dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra along the inclusion
\iota_{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
induces the inclusion functor
\mathrm{Inc}
. It clearly preserves all colimits as these are computed in the underlying category of chain complexes, and both categories are presentable, thus it admits a right adjoint
\mathrm{Sub}
.
∎

Remark 2.7
.

The functor
\mathrm{Sub}
is given by taking the maximal dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-subcoalgebra of a given dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebra. It generalizes taking the maximal conilpotent subcoalgebra of a non-necessarily conilpotent coalgebra.

Proposition 2.8
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumption
1
. The inclusion functor

\mathrm{Inc}:\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\longrightarrow\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}

is fully faithful. Therefore dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras are a coreflective subcategory of dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras.

Proof.

It is obviously faithful, as it does not change the underlying chain complex. Since the inclusion
\iota_{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
is a monomorphism, it is also full, as any morphism of dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras between two dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras is also a morphism of dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras.
∎

2.3.
The algebraic inclusion-restriction square

When we intertwine the restriction and the inclusion adjunctions with the two versions of Koszul duality induced by the classical bar-cobar and the complete bar-cobar adjunctions, we obtain a commuting square of adjoint functors.

Proposition 2.9
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumption
1
. The square of adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg},}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c\mathrm{Abs}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sub}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

commutes in the following sense: the compositions of the left adjoints from the top right to the bottom left of the square are naturally isomorphic.

Proof.

Let us check that the diagram commutes. First, there is a natural isomorphism

\mathrm{S}(\mathcal{P})(V)\cong\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P}^{*})(V),

for any dg module
V
, as the restriction functor commutes with the forgetful functors of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras and dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebras to dg modules. This gives a natural isomorphism of graded
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebras

c\mathrm{Abs}\circ\Omega_{\pi}(D)\cong\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}\circ\mathrm{Inc}(D)\penalty\ ,

for any dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra
D
. It can be checked that this isomorphism commutes with the differentials, as the restriction of both differentials to the generators
D
is given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}(n)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}D^{\otimes n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathcal{P}(n)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}D^{\otimes n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\prod_{n\geq 0}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}(\mathcal{P}^{*}(n),D^{\otimes n})\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta_{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathcal{P}}(D)}

where
\Delta_{D}
is the structural morphism of
D
and
\pi:\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}
the canonical twisting morphism.
∎

2.4.
Homotopical inclusion-restriction squares.

The inclusion-restriction square is compatible with the model structures on these categories in the following sense.

Lemma 2.10
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumption
1
.

(1)

The inclusion adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sub}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is a Quillen adjunction, where the model structure on dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras is that of Proposition
1.15
, where weak equivalences are given by quasi-isomorphisms.

(2)

The restriction adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c\mathrm{Abs}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is a Quillen adjunction, where the model structure on dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebras is that of Proposition
1.19
, where weak equivalences are given by quasi-isomorphisms.

Proof.

It is clear that
\mathrm{Inc}
preserves cofibrations and weak equivalences since they are given by monomorphisms and quasi-isomorphisms on both sides. Similarly,
\mathrm{Res}
preserves fibrations and weak equivalences since they are given by epimorphisms and quasi-isomorphisms on both sides.
∎

Theorem 2.11

(The inclusion-restriction rectangle)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumption
1
. There is a rectangle of adjunctions of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{W.eq}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}],}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{W.eq}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}c\mathrm{Abs}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathsf{Sub}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

where the bar-cobar adjunction and the complete bar-cobar adjunction are adjoint equivalences of
\infty
-categories. This rectangle is commutative, meaning that there is a natural weak equivalence between the left adjoint functors from the top left to the bottom right.

Proof.

The existence of this rectangle and the fact that it descends to the
\infty
-categorical setting follows directly from Proposition
2.9
and Lemma
2.10
. Let us check that it is indeed commutative and let us construct a natural weak equivalence between the following two composites

\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}\circ\mathrm{Id}\circ\mathbb{L}c\mathrm{Abs}\simeq\mathrm{Inc}\circ\mathrm{Id}\circ\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\penalty\ .

We will omit non-derived identity functors in order to simplify the equations. The idea is to explicitly compute the left hand side. Let
A
be a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. First, we can take
\Omega_{\pi}\mathrm{B}_{\pi}A
as a functorial cofibrant resolution of
A
, thus the derived functor
\mathbb{L}c\mathrm{Abs}(A)
can be computed as

\mathbb{L}c\mathrm{Abs}(A)\simeq\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}\circ\mathrm{Inc}\circ\mathrm{B}_{\pi}(A)\penalty\ .

Therefore the left hand side is given by

\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}\circ\mathbb{L}c\mathrm{Abs}(A)\simeq\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}\circ\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}\circ\mathrm{Inc}\circ\mathrm{B}_{\pi}(A)\penalty\ ,

and the counit

\epsilon_{\mathrm{Inc}\penalty\ \circ\penalty\ \mathrm{B}_{\pi}(A)}:\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}\circ\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}\circ\mathrm{Inc}\circ\mathrm{B}_{\pi}(A)\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathrm{Inc}\circ\mathrm{B}_{\pi}(A)

provides us with the natural weak equivalence we were looking for, as it is a natural quasi-isomorphism.
∎

Notation 2.12
.

In order to relieve notational burden, we will from now on omit the non-derived identity functors that appear in Theorem
2.11
and its consequences, if there are no ambiguities.

2.5.
The
\infty
-categorical bar-cobar adjunction

Using the commutative rectangle of Theorem
2.11
, we obtain an adjunction at the
\infty
-categorical level between dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras and their Koszul dual dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras. As we will explain in Section
7
, this adjunction is a generalization of Lurie’s adjunction between augmented monoids and coaugmented comonoids constructed in
[
43
, Theorem 5.2.2.17.]
.

Definition 2.13

(The
\infty
-categorical bar construction relative to
\mathcal{P}
)
.

The
\infty
-categorical
bar construction

\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{P}}
is the functor

\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{P}}\coloneqq\mathrm{Inc}\circ\mathrm{B}_{\pi}:\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\longrightarrow\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]

from the
\infty
-category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras localized at quasi-isomorphisms to the
\infty
-category of dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras localized at quasi-isomorphisms.

Definition 2.14

(The
\infty
-categorical cobar construction relative to
\mathcal{P}
)
.

The
\infty
-categorical
cobar construction

\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{P}}
is the functor

\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{P}}\coloneqq\mathrm{Res}\circ\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}:\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]

from the
\infty
-category of dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras localized at quasi-isomorphisms to the
\infty
-category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras localized at quasi-isomorphisms.

Remark 2.15
.

Strictly speaking, the functor
\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{P}}
is given as
\mathrm{Res}\circ\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Id}\circ\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}
. However, the image of the complete cobar functor is always cofibrant in the category of complete dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebras with the transferred model structure; thus the derived identity functor
\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Id}
is the identity functor and therefore we omit it for simplicity.

Theorem 2.16
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumption
1
. There is an adjunction of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between the
\infty
-category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras localized at quasi-isomorphisms and the
\infty
-category of dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras localized at quasi-isomorphisms.

Proof.

Follows immediately from Theorem
2.11
.
∎

Remark 2.17
.

This adjunction also agrees with the adjunction constructed in
[
36
]
over a field of characteristic zero. In general, there are two variants of this adjunction over a positive characteristic field and
op.cit.
considers one of them. See Subsection
5.4
.

3.
Point-set and
\infty
-categorical bar-cobar adjunctions

The goal of this section is to compare the point-set operadic bar-cobar adjunction of paragraph
1.1.6
with the
\infty
-categorical enhanced bar-cobar adjunction constructed in
[
22
]
. This latter bar-cobar adjunction goes from algebras over an (enriched)
\infty
-operad and (conilpotent, divided powers) coalgebras over its operadic bar construction. Contrary to what one would expect, the former adjunction does not present the latter. This is due to the failure of rectification statements for conilpotent coalgebras over a cooperad, as pointed out in
[
18
, Section 5.3]
, which follows from
[
23
, Chapter 6, Remark 2.6.7]
. Nevertheless, we construct a way to compare these two adjunctions and explain what the underlying chain complexes of each of these functors are.

3.1.
The enhanced bar-cobar adjunction of Francis–Gaitsgory

In this subsection, we recall some of the results in
[
22
, Section 3]
. Let
\mathscr{P}
be an augmented (enriched)
\infty
-operad in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
. The augmentation map
\epsilon:\mathscr{P}\longrightarrow\mathbb{1}
induces an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{indec}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{triv}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between the
\infty
-category of
\mathscr{P}
-algebras and the underlying
\infty
-category of chain complexes over
\Bbbk
. The functor
\mathbf{triv}
endows any chain complex with a trivial
\mathscr{P}
-algebra structure and its left adjoint
\mathbf{indec}
is the functor of derived indecomposables of a
\mathscr{P}
-algebra. The
homology
of
\mathbf{indec}
is called the (topological) André–Quillen homology, see
[
6
,
44
]
. This adjunction has a universal property: it exhibits
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
as the stabilization of the
\infty
-category of
\mathscr{P}
-algebras
[
7
]
. When
\mathscr{P}
is the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-operad in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
, this adjunction coincides with a particular case of the cotangent complex (see
[
43
, Section 7.3]
and in particular
[
43
, Remark 7.3.0.1]
).

Let us explain how the enhanced bar-cobar adjunction appears from the above data. The adjunction
\mathbf{indec}\dashv\mathbf{triv}
induces a comonad
\mathbf{T}:=\mathbf{indec}\circ\mathbf{triv}
on
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
. By its universal property, the adjunction
\mathbf{indec}\dashv\mathbf{triv}
factors through the
\infty
-category of coalgebras over the comonad
\mathbf{T}
, and we get

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \mathbf{Alg}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 30.0pt\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{T}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{indec}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{triv}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{cofree}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\Omega}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{U}}

where right adjoints are drawn on top and on the right. Moreover, the comonad
\mathbf{T}
can be explicitly identified.

Proposition 3.1
.

There is a natural weak equivalence of
\infty
-categorical comonads

\mathbf{T}\simeq\mathbf{S}(\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P}))(-)\simeq\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P})(n)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}(-)^{\otimes n}

between the comonad
\mathbf{T}
induced by the adjunction
\mathbf{indec}\dashv\mathbf{triv}
and the comonad given by the Schur functor of the
\infty
-cooperad
\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P})
.

Proof.

Let us compute the composition
\mathbf{indec}\dashv\mathbf{triv}
. Let
V
be a chain complex. Since the
\infty
-category of
\mathscr{P}
-algebras is monadic, any
\mathscr{P}
-algebra admits a resolution which is the geometric realization of a simplicial object made of free
\mathscr{P}
-algebras. In particular, so does the
\mathscr{P}
-algebra
\mathbf{triv}(V)
, which can be obtain as the geometric realization

\mathbf{triv}(V)\simeq\left|\penalty\ \mathbf{bar}(\mathbf{S}(\mathscr{P}),\mathbf{S}(\mathscr{P}),\mathbf{triv}(V))_{\bullet}\penalty\ \right|\penalty\ ,

where on the right hand side we consider the geometric realization of the simplicial object

\hskip-21.33955pt\small{\hbox to394.35pt{\vbox to28.45pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 197.1734pt\lower-13.9748pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

{}{}}

{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}{}{}{{{}{}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}{{}}{}{{{{}}}{{{}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 239.8 5.71 L 210.19 5.71}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{-1.0}{151.70702pt}{4.12498pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\penalty\ .

Since
\mathbf{indec}
commutes with colimits, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbf{indec}\circ\mathbf{triv}(V)

\displaystyle\simeq\left|\penalty\ \mathbf{indec}\left(\mathbf{bar}(\mathbf{S}(\mathscr{P}),\mathbf{S}(\mathscr{P}),\mathbf{triv}(V))_{\bullet}\right)\penalty\ \right|

\displaystyle\simeq\left|\penalty\ \left(\mathbf{bar}(\mathbf{id},\mathbf{S}(\mathscr{P}),\mathbf{triv}(V))_{\bullet}\right)\penalty\ \right|

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbf{S}(\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P}))(V)\penalty\ ,

where
\mathbf{id}
is the identity endofunctor. The last equivalence follows from the fact that
\mathbf{S}(-)
also commutes with colimits, and that the structure maps of
\mathbf{triv}(V)
are all trivial.
∎

Hence we get an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P})}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\Omega}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

called the
enhanced bar-cobar adjunction
in
[
22
]
, between
\mathscr{P}
-algebras and conilpotent divided powers
\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P})
-coalgebras in the sense of Definition
1.29
. This adjunction is the universal factorization of the indecomposables-trivial adjunction over coalgebras over a comonad.

Remark 3.2
.

The original Francis–Gaitsgory conjecture, disproved in
[
36
]
, stated that this adjunction is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories when one restricts the enhanced bar construction to
pro-nilpotent

\mathscr{P}
-algebras. See
[
22
, Section 3.4]
for the original statement.

3.2.
A comparison between strict and
\infty
-categorical conilpotent coalgebras

Let us start by explaining why the point-set operadic bar-cobar adjunction cannot present its
\infty
-categorical analogue given by the enhanced bar-cobar adjunction. Concretely, one would like to say that the following composite adjunction

(11)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ [\mathsf{W}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathrm{Id}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

coincides with the enhanced bar-cobar adjunction

(12)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\Omega}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

However, if this was true, then because the adjunction (
11
) is a composite of an equivalence together with a left Bousfield localization, it would imply that
\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})
-coalgebras are a reflective sub-
\infty
-category of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras. However, as pointed out in
[
18
, Theorem 5.3.1]
, this cannot be the case by the counterexample given in
[
23
, Chapter 6, Remark 2.6.7]
, where one takes
\mathcal{P}
to be the commutative operad
\mathcal{C}om
.

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumptions
1
. There is an adjunction of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{loose}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{strict}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras up to quasi-isomorphism and
\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})
-coalgebras, where
\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})
is the underlying enriched
\infty
-cooperad of
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
.

Proof.

This adjunction follows from the fact that cofree-forgetful adjunction, at the point-set level, between dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras and chain complexes, induces the comonad
\mathrm{S}(\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P})(-)
on the underlying category of chain complexes. This
1
-comonad presents the
\infty
-categorical comonad
\mathbf{S}(\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P}))(-)
on
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
, since by Remark
1.25
the conilpotent dg cooperad
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
presents the enriched
\infty
-cooperad
\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})
. Hence, by the (dual) universal property of the Eilenberg-Moore category, there exists such an adjunction.
∎

The above adjunction is not, in general, an equivalence of
\infty
-categories, which prevents us from giving point-set models for the enhanced bar-cobar adjunction. The core issue is that the cofree-forgetful adjunction between dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras and chain complexes is not comonadic in the
\infty
-categorical sense in general. A characterization of the
\infty
-category of dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras up to quasi-isomorphisms is the following: it is the localization of the
\infty
-category of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras with respect to maps which are sent by
\mathrm{B}_{\pi}
to a quasi-isomorphism. In other words, with respect to
\mathcal{P}
-algebra maps which induce an equivalence in (topological) André–Quillen homology.

3.3.
The operadic bar-cobar adjunction and the enhanced bar-cobar adjunction

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumptions
1
. We show that the composition of the point-set adjunction (
11
) together with the adjunction of Proposition
3.3
given indeed the enhanced bar-cobar adjunction (
12
). See also
[
18
, Section 5.3]
for similar statements.

Theorem 3.4
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumptions
1
. The
\infty
-categorical adjunction induced by the bar-cobar adjunction with respect to
\pi
composed with the left Bousfield localization with respect to quasi-isomorphisms and with the comparison adjunction of Proposition
3.3

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ [\mathsf{W}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{loose}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{strict}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is naturally equivalent to the
\infty
-categorical enhanced bar-cobar adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\Omega}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Proof.

The dg operad
\mathcal{P}
is in particular augmented, the augmentation induces a Quillen adjunction
\mathrm{indec}\dashv\mathrm{triv}
at the point-set level. The derived adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Ch}(\Bbbk)\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{triv}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}\mathrm{indec}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

presents the adjunction
\mathbf{indec}\dashv\mathbf{triv}
of the underlying
\infty
-operad of
\mathcal{P}
. Moreover, both adjunctions in the above theorem are universal factorizations of the adjunction
\mathbf{indec}\dashv\mathbf{triv}
through the same
\infty
-category of coalgebras. Therefore, since they satisfy the same universal property, they must coincide. See e.g.
[
32
]
for the
\infty
-categorical version of the universal property of the Eilenberg–Moore category of algebras over a monad. ∎

Remark 3.5
.

Over a positive characteristic field, Theorem
3.4
also holds in the following, more general context: given a dg operad
\mathcal{P}
which is
\mathbb{S}
-projective, the quasi-planar bar-cobar adjunction of
[
28
, Section 3.14]
composed with the Bousfield left localization of
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{P}\otimes\mathcal{E})
as in
[
28
, Proposition 48]
also fits in the same diagram, where at the
\infty
-categorical level we also consider the enhanced bar-cobar adjunction with respect of the underlying enriched
\infty
-operad of
\mathcal{P}
.

4.
A dual version of the
\infty
-categorical enhanced bar-cobar adjunction

In this section, we introduce a dual version of the enhanced bar-cobar adjunction constructed in
[
22
]
, which is constructed by considering the universal factorization of the derived primitives functor of a given class of coalgebras (not necessarily conilpotent) through algebras over a monad. We prove that this results in an adjunction between coalgebras over an (enriched)
\infty
-operad and algebras over its dual
\infty
-cooperad. Moreover, and contrary to what happens with the enhanced bar-cobar adjunction, we give point-set models for this new construction.

4.1.
A complete version of the enhanced bar-cobar adjunction

Let
\mathscr{P}
be an augmented (enriched)
\infty
-operad in
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
. There is an associated
\infty
-category of
\mathscr{P}
-coalgebras, whose objecs are the coalgebras (non-conilpotent, without divided powers) defined by the operations in
\mathscr{P}
. For example, if
\mathscr{P}
is the
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-operad or the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-operad, then
\mathscr{P}
-coalgebras correspond to
\mathbb{E}_{1}
- or
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-coalgebras in the sense of
[
43
]
. We refer to Subsection
1.2.5
or
[
48
, Section 3]
for more details about this definition.

The augmentation map
\epsilon:\mathscr{P}\longrightarrow\mathbb{1}
induces another adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{prim}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{triv}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

this time between the
\infty
-category of
\mathscr{P}
-coalgebras and the derived
\infty
-category of chain complexes over
\Bbbk
. The functor
\mathbf{triv}
endows any chain complex with a trivial
\mathscr{P}
-coalgebra structure and its right adjoint
\mathbf{prim}
is the functor of derived primitives of a
\mathscr{P}
-coalgebra. Its homology groups should be understood as (topological) coAndré-Quillen homology groups for types of coalgebras, of which (topological) coHochschild homology groups in the sense of
[
33
]
and
[
8
]
are particular examples. See, in particular,
[
8
, Definition 2.10]
.

The adjunction
\mathbf{triv}\dashv\mathbf{prim}
induces a monad on the derived
\infty
-category of chain complexes, which we denote by
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathscr{P}}=\mathbf{prim}\circ\mathbf{triv}
. Thus, the derived primitives adjunction factors universally through the
\infty
-category of
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathscr{P}}
-algebras, and we obtain the following triangle of adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 12.0pt\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathscr{P}}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 30.0pt\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{free}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathscr{P}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{triv}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\widehat{\mathrm{B}}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{prim}}

where left adjoints are drawn on top and on the right. In particular, this gives a universal adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbf{Q}_{\mathscr{P}}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\widehat{\mathrm{B}}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

which factors the derived primitives-trivial structure adjunction, which we call the
complete enhanced bar-cobar adjunction
. Although the triangle constructed above is dual to the one constructed in Subsection
3.1
, Proposition
3.1
does not a priori dualize and we can not identify the monad
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathscr{P}}
right away. The reason is that the cosimplicial diagram that resolves a generic
\mathscr{P}
-coalgebra is made of cofree
\mathscr{P}
-coalgebras, which unlike algebras, admit no easy description. Nevertheless, using point-set models, we will be able to present the
\infty
-categorical
\mathbf{triv}\dashv\mathbf{prim}
adjunction, its induced monad and thus the
\infty
-category of
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathscr{P}}
, which is going to be identified with the
\infty
-category of algebras over the
\infty
-cooperad
\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P})
in the sense of Definition
1.34
.

4.2.
Absolute algebras and limit towers of
\infty
-categories

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent dg cooperad which satisfies Assumptions
1
. It can be written as the colimit of the following tower of cooperads

\tau^{\leq 1}\mathcal{C}\rightarrowtail\tau^{\leq 2}\mathcal{C}\rightarrowtail\cdots\rightarrowtail\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{C}\rightarrowtail\cdots\rightarrowtail\colim_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{C}\cong\mathcal{C}\penalty\ ,

where
\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{C}
is the truncation of
\mathcal{C}
at arities strictly larger than
k
, for all
k\geq 1
. This tower induces an
arity-filtration
on any dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
A
, whose successive quotients are given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{C})(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{C})(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A/\mathrm{F}^{k}A\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(i_{k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ulcorner}

where
i_{k}:\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}
is the canonical inclusion. Let us denote by

\upsilon_{A}:A\longrightarrow(\widehat{A})_{\mathrm{arity}}\coloneqq\lim_{k\in\mathbb{N}}A/\mathrm{F}^{k}A

the canonical map from
A
to its
arity-completion
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent dg cooperad which satisfies Assumptions
1
and let
A
be a dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra. The map

\upsilon_{A}:A\twoheadrightarrow\lim_{k\in\mathbb{N}}A/\mathrm{F}^{k}A

is a degree-wise epimorphism.

Proof.

The idea of the proof is completely analogous to
[
27
, Proposition 4.24]
. One must replace the weight filtration with the arity filtration in
[
27
, 4.20, 4.21, 4.22 and 4.23]
. See also
[
28
, Section 3.6]
for similar arguments.
∎

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent dg cooperad which satisfies Assumptions
1
and let
A
be a complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra. The map

\upsilon_{A}:A\xrightarrow{\simeq}\lim_{k\in\mathbb{N}}A/\mathrm{F}^{k}A

is an isomorphism, thus any complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra is arity-complete.

Proof.

By Lemma
4.1
, it suffices to show that
\upsilon_{A}
is a monomorphism. The key point is the following: since
\mathcal{C}
satisfies Assumptions
1
, then it is cogenerated in arities
\geq 2
. This implies that

\mathrm{F}^{k}A\subset\mathrm{W}^{k-1}A\penalty\ ,

since operations in arities
\leq k
can be of weight at most
k-1
. This, in turn, implies that

\bigcap_{k\geq 1}\mathrm{F}^{k}A\subset\bigcap_{k\geq 1}\mathrm{W}^{k-1}A=\{0\}\penalty\ ,

since we assumed
A
to be complete. Hence the kernel of the map
\upsilon_{A}
is zero and it is also a monomorphism. ∎

Therefore, any complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
A
can be written as a limit

A\cong\lim_{k\in\mathbb{N}}A/\mathrm{F}^{k}A\penalty\ .

Notice that
A/\mathrm{F}^{k}A
is the unit of the adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i_{k})_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i_{k})^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

induced by the inclusion
i_{k}:\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}
, that is, we have an isomorphism
(i_{k})^{*}(i_{k})_{!}(A)\cong A/\mathrm{F}^{k}A
for any complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
A
.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
k\geq 1
. There is an equivalence of categories

\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\cong\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{C}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ .

Proof.

The natural inclusion of monads

\iota_{\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{C}}:\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}^{k}\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{C}^{*}(n)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}(-)^{\otimes n}\hookrightarrow\prod_{n\geq 0}^{k}\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}(\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{C}(n),(-)^{\otimes n})\penalty\ ,

is in fact an isomorphisms since finite sums and products agree.
∎

Theorem 4.4
.

There is an adjunction of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{k\geq 1}^{h}\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{C}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{L}(i_{k})_{!}(-))_{k\geq 1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{k\geq 1}^{h}(i_{k})^{*}(-)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

where the left adjoint
(\mathbb{L}(i_{k})_{!}(-))_{k\geq 1}
is fully faithful.

Proof.

Up to the equivalence in Lemma
4.3
, the adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i_{k})_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(i_{k})^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is a Quillen adjunction for all
n\geq 1
, since the functor
(i_{n})^{*}
clearly preserves fibrations and quasi-isomorphisms. This induces a functor of
\infty
-categories

(\mathbb{L}(i_{k})_{!}(-))_{k\geq 1}:\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\longrightarrow\lim_{k\geq 1}\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{C}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\penalty\ .

The functor sends a complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra
A
to the collection
(\mathbb{L}(i_{k})_{!}(A))_{k\geq 1}
, which lies in the limit in the right hand side. It can be checked that it has a a right adjoint, which sends a collection
(A_{k}^{\prime})_{k\geq 1}
of
\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{C}^{*}
-algebras to their (homotopy) limit
\lim_{k\geq 1}^{h}((i_{k})^{*}(A_{k}^{\prime}))_{k\geq 1}
. Let
A
be a cofibrant complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebra, it can be written as a (1-categorical) limit

A\cong\lim_{k\in\mathbb{N}}(i_{k})^{*}(i_{k})_{!}(A)\penalty\ ,

where all the maps are surjections and all the algebras are fibrant. Hence it is also a homotopy limit. Furthermore, since
A
is cofibrant, the object
(i_{k})_{!}(A)
computes
\mathbb{L}(i_{k})_{!}(A)
. Therefore the unit of the
\infty
-categorical adjunction is a weak equivalence and
\mathbb{L}(i_{k})_{!}
is fully faithful.
∎

Remark 4.5

(Relationship with the Goodwillie tower)
.

The limit

\lim_{n\geq 1}\tau^{\leq n}\mathcal{C}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]

is precisely the limit of the Goodwillie tower of the
\infty
-category of dg
\mathcal{C}^{*}
-algebras, localized at quasi-isomorphisms, in the sense of
[
35
]
. See
[
35
, Section 6.1]
for more details.

Remark 4.6
.

There is a more general version of Theorem
4.4
, where one does not assume the conditions in Assumptions
1
. It gives an adjunction of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{\omega\geq 1}^{h}\mathscr{R}_{\omega}\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{L}(i_{\omega})_{!}(-))_{\omega\geq 1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{\omega\geq 1}^{h}(i_{\omega})^{*}(-)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

where
\mathscr{R}_{\omega}
denotes the
\omega
-stage of the coradical filtration of the conilpotent dg cooperad
\mathcal{C}
, where the left adjoint functor is again fully faithful. However, in general the weight filtration induced by the coradical filtration of
\mathcal{C}
and the arity filtration induced by the truncations of
\mathcal{C}
differ, and one neither identify the
\infty
-categories in this limit with those of algebras over operads nor compare it to the Goodwillie tower of the
\infty
-category of dg
\mathcal{C}^{*}
-algebras up to quasi-isomorphisms.

The limit considered in Theorem
4.4

\lim_{k\geq 1}\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{C}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]

is a priori taken in the
\infty
-category of presentable
\infty
-categories and right adjoints above
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
. It coincides with the limit in the
\infty
-category of presentable
\infty
-categories and right adjoints since the diagram is contractible and thus can also be computed in the
\infty
-category of
\infty
-categories.

Corollary 4.7
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent dg cooperad satisfying Assumptions
1
. The Quillen adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Ch}(\Bbbk)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{C})(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras, endowed with the model structure where weak equivalences are quasi-isomorphisms and fibrations degree-wise surjection, and the underlying category of chain complexes induces an adjunction at the level of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{C})(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

which is monadic.

Proof.

The result essentially follows from Theorem
4.4
. Indeed, the right hand side is a limit diagram of presentable
\infty
-categories and right adjoints over
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
, where all the functors are monadic in the
\infty
-categorical sense by Lemma
4.3
. This limit is therefore still monadic over
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
by
[
32
, Theorem 1.3]
. Since complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras up to quasi-isomorphisms are a coreflective
\infty
-subcategory, colimits are computed in the ambient
\infty
-category. Therefore the forgetful functor from complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras up to quasi-isomorphisms to
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
preserves geometric realizations of
\mathrm{U}
-split simplicial objects, and since it is obviously conservative, it is monadic by the Barr–Beck–Lurie theorem of
[
43
, Theorem 4.7.0.3.]
.
∎

Corollary 4.8
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent dg cooperad satisfying Assumptions
1
. There is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\simeq\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))

between complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras up to quasi-isomorphisms and algebras over the
\infty
-cooperad induced by the dg cooperad
\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

Since both
\infty
-categories are monadic over the
\infty
-category of
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
, it suffices by the Barr–Beck–Lurie theorem (see
[
43
, Corollary 4.7.3.16.]
) to show that the two monads are naturally weakly equivalent. This, again, is immediate, since the point-set dual Schur functor
\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{C})
preserves quasi-isomorphisms, and thus induces the
\infty
-categorical dual Schur functor of Paragraph
1.2.4
.
∎

4.3.
Identifying the monad via point-set models

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a reduced dg operad. Since we want to give point-set models for the
\infty
-category of coalgebras over its underlying
\infty
-operad, we need to further assume that is is
cofibrant
as an dg operad. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
\mathcal{P}=\Omega\mathcal{C}
for some reduced conilpotent dg cooperad
\mathcal{C}
. We further impose that
\mathcal{C}
satisfies Assumptions
1
. We are first going to identify the monad
\mathbf{Q}
introduced in Subsection
4.1
with the
\infty
-category of algebras over the cooperad
\mathcal{C}
, and then this will allows us to give point-set models for the complete enhanced bar-cobar adjunction.

In the context above, the dg operad
\Omega\mathcal{C}
is augmented, and the augmentation induces a Quillen adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Ch}(\Bbbk)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{triv}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{prim}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

at the point-set level between dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras and chain complexes, where dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras are endowed with a left transferred model structure along the cofree-forgetful adjunction, where cofibrations are given by monomorphisms and weak equivalences by quasi-isomorphisms. The functor
\mathrm{triv}
endows a chain complex with a trivial dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra structure and its adjoint
\mathrm{prim}
computes the primitive elements in a dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent dg cooperad satisfying Assumptions
1
. The derived adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathrm{prim}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{triv}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is a model for the
\infty
-categorical derived primitives-trivial adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\Omega\mathcal{C}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{prim}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{triv}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between coalgebras over the underlying
\infty
-operad of
\Omega\mathcal{C}
and the
\infty
-category of chain complexes over
\Bbbk
.

Proof.

The main key point in the proof is the comparison between the
\infty
-categories of dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras up to quasi-isomorphisms and coalgebras defined over the underlying
\infty
-operad of
\Omega\mathcal{C}
. In
[
48
]
, we showed that since the dg operad
\Omega\mathcal{C}
is
cofibrant as an operad
, there is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\Omega\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\simeq\mathbf{Coalg}_{\Omega\mathcal{C}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ .

Under this equivalence, it is straightforward to see that the (derived) functor
\mathrm{triv}
and the functor
\mathbf{triv}
agree, and thus the two adjunction are naturally equivalent.
∎

Theorem 4.10
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent dg cooperad satisfying Assumptions
1
. There is a natural weak equivalence of monads

\mathbf{Q}_{\Omega\mathcal{C}}\simeq\widehat{\mathbf{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{C})\simeq\prod_{n\geq 0}\left[\mathcal{C}(n),(-)^{\otimes n}\right]^{h\mathbb{S}_{n}}

between the monad
\mathbf{Q}_{\Omega\mathcal{C}}
introduced in §
4.1
, which factors the derived primitives-trivial adjunction of
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras, and the monad obtained by applying the
\infty
-categorical dual Schur functor to the underlying
\infty
-cooperad of
\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

4.9
, we can compute the monad induced by the
\mathbf{triv}\dashv\mathbf{prim}
adjunction using the adjunction
\mathrm{triv}\dashv\mathbb{R}\mathrm{prim}
. For any chain complex
V
, we have that

\mathbb{R}\mathrm{prim}\circ\mathrm{triv}(V)\simeq\mathrm{prim}\circ\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}(V)\simeq\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{C})(V)\penalty\ ,

since the structure of
V
is trivial. The latter functor models
\widehat{\mathbf{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{C})
with its monad structure.
∎

Remark 4.11
.

If we start with a cofibrant dg operad
\mathcal{P}
in general, we can always replace it by the cofibrant operad
\Omega\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
, so
\mathcal{C}
can be taken to be
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
in the above theorem. Since by Remark
1.25
, the point-set operadic bar construction presents Lurie’s bar construction, we also get that, up to equivalence, the monad
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathcal{P}}
can be identified to the monad obtained by applying the
\infty
-categorical dual Schur functor to the
\infty
-operad
\mathbf{bar}\mathcal{P}
.

Remark 4.12
.

Another consequence of Theorem
4.10
is that the totalization of the cosimplicial object

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{L}(\Omega\mathcal{C})^{\circ 3}(\mathbf{triv}(V))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{L}(\Omega\mathcal{C})^{\circ 2}(\mathbf{triv}(V))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{L}(\Omega\mathcal{C})(\mathbf{triv}(V))}

coincides with
\widehat{\mathbf{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{C})(V)
for any chain complex
V
, where here
\mathbf{L}(\Omega\mathcal{C})
denotes the
\infty
-categorical cofree
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebra comonad. Indeed, using analogous arguments as in the proof of Proposition
3.1
, it can be seen that the totalization of this cosimplicial object agrees with the value of the monad induced by the adjunction
\mathbf{triv}\circ\mathbf{prim}
associated to the
\infty
-category of
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras.

Theorem 4.13
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a conilpotent dg cooperad satisfying Assumptions
1
. The
\infty
-categorical adjunction induced by the complete bar-cobar adjunction with respect to
\iota
composed with the right Bousfield localization with respect to quasi-isomorphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{W}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Id}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is naturally weakly equivalent to the
\infty
-categorical complete enhanced bar-cobar adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\Omega\mathcal{C}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\widehat{\mathrm{B}}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Proof.

Directly follows from
Theorem

4.10
together with
Corollary

4.8
, using the fact that both are universal factorizations of the adjunction
\mathbf{triv}\dashv\mathbf{prim}
for
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras.
∎

Remark 4.14
.

Over a positive characteristic field, Theorem
3.4
should hold for any
quasi-planar
conilpotent dg cooperad
\mathcal{C}
satisfying
1
, where one replaces complete dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras with
qp-complete
dg
\mathcal{C}
-algebras in the sense of
[
28
, Sections 3]
.

5.
The
\infty
-categorical inclusion-restriction squares

In this section, we put together the previous pieces to construct an
\infty
-categorical inclusion-restriction square, similar to the point-set inclusion-restriction square constructed in Section
2
. We compare the resulting adjunction with the one constructed by Heuts in
[
36
]
. Finally, we discuss how these results could be extended in positive characteristic.

5.1.
Comparing
\infty
-categories of coalgebras

We establish different comparison functors and equivalences between point-set conilpotent (divided powers) coalgebras over a cooperad, conilpotent (divided powers) coalgebras over a
\infty
-cooperad, non-necessarily conilpotent (divided powers) coalgebras over a
\infty
-cooperad in the sense of
[
36
, Appendix A]
, and coalgebras over an
\infty
-operad, in the sense of
[
48
, Section 3]
. These results shall be useful in the subsequent sections.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a reduced
\infty
-cooperad. There is an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathscr{C}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathscr{C}^{*}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between (divided powers)
\mathscr{C}
-coalgebras in the sense of
[
36
, Appendix A]
and
\mathscr{C}^{*}
-coalgebras in the sense of Definition
1.36
.

Furthermore, if we assume that either
\mathscr{C}
is non-symmetric or that
\Bbbk
is a characteristic zero field, then this adjunction adjunction is an equivalence when
\mathscr{C}
has arity-wise finite dimensional homology.

Proof.

Both
\infty
-categories are defined as a pullbacks along the inclusion of
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
into
\mathbf{pro}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))
of
\infty
-categories of coalgebras in
\mathbf{pro}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))
, so it suffices to compare the latter. Divided powers
\mathscr{C}
-coalgebras in the sense of
[
36
, Appendix A]
in
\mathbf{pro}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))
are coalgebras over the comonad (keeping the notation of
op.cit.
) given by

\widehat{p\mathbf{Sym}}_{\mathscr{C}}=\lim_{k}p\left(\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}^{k}\left(\mathscr{C}(n)\otimes(-)^{\otimes n}\right)_{h\mathbb{S}_{n}}\right)\penalty\ ,

where
p
stands for the prolongation functor that sends an endofunctor of
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
to an endofunctor of
\mathbf{pro}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))
. Very similarly,
\mathscr{C}^{*}
-coalgebras in
\mathbf{pro}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))
are coalgebras over the comonad

\widehat{\mathbf{S}}^{c}_{\mathrm{pro}}(\mathscr{C}^{*})=\lim_{k}p\left(\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}^{k}\left[\mathscr{C}^{*}(n),(-)^{\otimes n}\right]^{h\mathbb{S}_{n}}\right)\penalty\ ,

where
[-,-]
refers to the internal hom of
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
. For any
k\geq 1
, there is a map of endofunctors

\iota_{\mathscr{C}}^{\leq k}:\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}^{k}\left(\mathscr{C}(n)\otimes(-)^{\otimes n}\right)_{h\mathbb{S}_{n}}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}^{k}\left[\mathscr{C}^{*}(n),(-)^{\otimes n}\right]^{h\mathbb{S}_{n}}

given by the natural map
V^{*}\otimes W\longrightarrow[V,W]
together with the double dual map and the norm map from coinvariants to invariants. It induces a map of comonads

\lim_{k}p(\iota_{\mathscr{C}}^{\leq k}):\widehat{p\mathbf{Sym}}_{\mathscr{C}}\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathbf{S}}^{c}_{\mathrm{pro}}(\mathscr{C}^{*})\penalty\ .

And this morphism of comonads in
\mathbf{pro}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))
induces the desired adjunction, whose pullback is the one stated in the lemma.

Moreover, if
\mathscr{C}
has arity-wise finite dimensional homology, the maps
\iota_{\mathscr{C}}^{\leq k}
are all equivalences since
\mathscr{C}(n)
has total finite dimensional homology and since the norm maps are all equivalences. Hence the induced map of comonads is an equivalence and the two
\infty
-categories of coalgebras are equivalent.
∎

Remark 5.2
.

There are two main differences between the definition of coalgebras considered in
[
36
, Appendix A]
and the definition considered in
[
48
]
:

(1)

When working in a general context, the first one encodes
divided powers
non-necessarily conilpotent types of coalgebras, whereas the second does not involve
divided powers
.

(2)

The first has an extra finiteness condition that the later does not have in the non-finite dimensional case. Indeed, assume that
\mathscr{C}(n)
is infinite dimensional. Then, at a
given arity
, decompositions in the first definition land on the tensor product

\Delta_{C}(n):C\longrightarrow\mathscr{C}(n)\otimes C^{\otimes n}\penalty\ ,

hence are finite sums of pure tensors, whereas in the second definition, they land on the internal hom, so "infinite sums of pure tensors" are allowed.

The previous preliminary result allows us to construct a "forgetful functor" or "inclusion functor" from conilpotent divided powers coalgebras over an enriched
\infty
-cooperad and coalgebras over its linear dual
\infty
-operad. Note, however, that it is not a priori obvious whether this
\infty
-categorical "inclusion functor" is fully faithful or not, as explained in
[
36
, Remark 11.2]
. This fully faithfulness was conjectured in
[
23
, Conjecture 2.8.4]
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a reduced
\infty
-cooperad. There is an adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathscr{C}}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathscr{C}^{*}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Sub}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between conilpotent (divided powers)
\mathscr{C}
-coalgebras and
\mathscr{C}^{*}
-coalgebras.

Proof.

We compose the adjunction constructed between conilpotent divided powers coalgebras over
\mathscr{C}
in the sense of Definition
1.29
and divided powers coalgebras over
\mathscr{C}
in the sense of
[
36
, Appendix A]
, constructed in
[
36
, Section 7]
with the adjunction of Lemma
5.1
.
∎

Remark 5.4
.

The left adjoint functor could also be constructed inductively like in
[
36
, Section 7]
, using the analoguous decomposition of the
\infty
-category of
\mathscr{C}^{*}
-coalgebras as a limit, which holds because of
[
48
, Proposition 3.16]
.

Finally, we put together the comparison functors constructed so far to obtain a commuting triangle of functors between the point-set version of conilpotent divided powers coalgebras, their
\infty
-categorical counterparts and the
\infty
-category of all coalgebras of that given type.

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumptions
1
. The following triangle of adjunctions of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\hskip 12.0pt}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\simeq\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{cobar}(\mathcal{P}^{*})}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{loose}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{strict}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Sub}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathrm{Sub}}

commutes, where left adjoints are drawn on the top and on the left.

Proof.

The vertical right adjunction is given by Lemma
5.3
, where
(\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P}))^{*}\simeq\mathbf{cobar}(\mathcal{P}^{*})
since
\mathcal{P}
satisfies Assumptions
1
. The top horizontal adjunction is constructed in Proposition
3.3
. The diagonal adjunction is given by the derived inclusion-maximal conilpotent subcoalgebra Quillen adjunction of Lemma
2.10
, under the equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\simeq\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{cobar}(\mathcal{P}^{*})}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ ,

which is given by Theorem
1.37
.

If we compse the cofree-forgetful adjunction between
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras and chain complexes with either the
\mathrm{Inc}\dashv\mathbb{R}\mathrm{Sub}
adjunction or the
\mathbf{Inc}\dashv\mathbf{Sub}
adjunction, we get, in both cases, the same comonad
\mathbf{S}(\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P}))
on chain complexes. Hence, since the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))
is precisely the category of coalgebras over this comonad, it is the terminal object in the
\infty
-category of adjunctions that produce the comonad
\mathbf{S}(\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P}))
, and thus there is an essentially unique adjunction between
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras and
\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))
which makes this triangle commute, which is
\mathbf{loose}\dashv\mathbf{strict}
.
∎

5.2.
The
\infty
-categorical inclusion-restriction square

We use the point-set version of the inclusion-restriction square of Theorem
2.11
together with the comparisons established in
Theorem

3.4
and
Theorem

4.13
to construct an
\infty
-categorical version of the inclusion-restriction square. We suspect that a result like this should holds in a much more general context, e.g. for enriched
\infty
-operads in any compactly rigidly generated stable symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category.

Theorem 5.6
.

Let
\mathscr{P}
be a reduced
\infty
-operad with arity-wise bounded and finite dimensional homology groups. The following diagram of adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P})}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)),}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathscr{P}^{*}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{cobar}(\mathscr{P}^{*})}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathrm{B}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{cAbs}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\Omega}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\widehat{\mathrm{B}}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Sub}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

commutes in the following sense: left adjoints from the top left to the bottom right are naturally weakly equivalent.

Proof.

The two horizontal adjunctions correspond to the enhanced bar-cobar adjunction, defined in Subsection
3.1
and its dual version, defined in Subsection
4.1
. The right vertical adjunction is given by Lemma
5.3
and the left vertical adjunction is induced the canonical morphism of monads

\iota_{\mathscr{P}}:\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}\left(\mathscr{P}(n)\otimes(-)^{\otimes n}\right)_{h\mathbb{S}_{n}}\longrightarrow\prod_{n\geq 0}\left[\mathscr{P}^{*}(n),(-)^{\otimes n}\right]^{h\mathbb{S}_{n}}

in a completely analogous way to Proposition
2.3
. We are left to check that this square of adjunctions does, indeed, commute.

The first part of the proof consists in showing that any reduced
\infty
-operad
\mathscr{P}
with arity-wise bounded and finite dimensional homology groups admits a point-set model
\mathcal{P}
, which is a dg operad stasifying Assumptions
1
, so that we can apply Theorem
2.11
to
\mathcal{P}
.

Since the
\infty
-category of
\infty
-operads is presented by the category of dg operads localized at quasi-isomorphisms
[
39
]
, there exists a point-set model
\mathcal{Q}
for this
\infty
-operad. Now, let us explain why, for any choice of
\mathcal{Q}
, we can construct another model
\mathcal{P}
which is a reduced dg operad such that each
\mathcal{P}(n)
is a bounded and degree-wise finite dimensional chain complex for all
n\geq 0
. First, we can apply the homotopy transfer theorem to
\mathcal{Q}
to get an up-to-homotopy operad structure on
\mathrm{H}_{*}(\mathcal{Q})
(see
[
58
,
26
]
). This gives in particular a weak equivalence of conilpotent dg cooperads between
\mathrm{B}\mathrm{H}_{*}(\mathcal{Q})
and
\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{Q})
. Therefore,
\Omega\mathrm{B}\mathrm{H}_{*}(\mathcal{Q})
is quasi-isomorphic to
\Omega\mathrm{B}(\mathcal{Q})
and thus to
\mathcal{Q}
. Moreover, each
\Omega\mathrm{B}\mathrm{H}_{*}(\mathcal{Q})(n)
is a bounded and degree-wise finite dimensional chain complex since the homology of
\mathcal{Q}(n)
is by assumption bounded and finite dimensional. Therefore we can set
\mathcal{P}=\Omega\mathrm{B}\mathrm{H}_{*}(\mathcal{Q})
, which satisfies Assumptions
1
, and apply Theorem
2.11
.

In this way, by applying Theorem
2.11
, together with Proposition
5.5
and Theorems
3.4
and
4.13
, we get the following natural equivalences:

\mathbf{Inc}\circ\bm{\mathrm{B}}^{\mathbf{enh}}\simeq\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{P}}\simeq\mathrm{Inc}\circ\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\simeq\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}\circ\mathbb{L}\mathrm{Id}\circ\mathbb{L}c\mathrm{Abs}\simeq\bm{\widehat{\mathrm{B}}}^{\mathbf{enh}}\circ\mathbf{cAbs}\penalty\ ,

making the diagram commute, where we use the fact that the Quillen adjunction of Proposition
2.3
is a point-set model for its
\infty
-categorical counterpart, using directly Corollary
4.8
.
∎

Remark 5.7
.

It follows from the above that the
\infty
-categorical bar-cobar adjunction which follows from Theorem
2.16
admits the following
\infty
-categorical description
