\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{P}}\simeq\mathbf{Inc}\circ\bm{\mathrm{B}}^{\mathbf{enh}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{P}}\simeq\mathbf{Res}\circ\bm{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\mathbf{enh}}\penalty\ ,

and, vice-versa, these two composites admit explicit point-set models given in Theorem
2.16
.

5.3.
A comparison with the adjunction constructed by Heuts

We compare the composite adjunction given by Theorem
5.6
with the adjunction considered in
[
36
]
, which we first recall. This comparison shall also be useful in Section
6
in order to compare our results with those obtained in
op.cit.
Heuts considers more generally operads and cooperads in a presentably symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-category
\mathbf{C}
. We work in the special case of
\mathbf{C}=\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
.

Theorem 5.8
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumptions
1
. The adjunction of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\text{$\mathcal{P}$}}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between the
\infty
-category of dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras localized at quasi-isomorphisms and the
\infty
-category of dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras localized at quasi-isomorphisms coincides with the adjunction considered in
[
36
, Theorem 2.1]
for the underlying
\infty
-operad of
\mathcal{P}
, which is defined as the following composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\Omega}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Sub}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{P}
satisfies Assumptions
1
, we can use Lemma
5.1
to get an equivalence between the target coalgebra category considered in
[
36
]
and ours:

\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\simeq\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{cobar}(\mathcal{P}^{*})}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ .

Then, the result essentially follows from the respective definitions, using the Theorem
3.4
and Proposition
5.5
.
∎

5.4.
About the positive characteristic situation

The constructions of
[
36
]
are valid in any presentable stable symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathbf{C}
. Let
\mathscr{P}
be an
\infty
-operad enriched in
\mathbf{C}
. In this setting, the adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathbf{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P})}^{\mathbf{pd},\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{C})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P})}^{\mathbf{pd}}(\mathbf{C})\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\Omega}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Sub}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

land in
\infty
-categories of
\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P})
-coalgebras which now has a priori divided powers operations. These prevent the adjunction in Lemma
5.1
from being an equivalence.

Notation 5.9
.

We add the superscript
\mathbf{pd}
in this subsection to keep track of divided powers operations.

In this setting, we expect the above adjunction to fit in a more general inclusion-restriction square of the form

(13)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathscr{P}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P})}^{\mathbf{pd},\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)),}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathscr{P}^{*}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{cobar}(\mathscr{P}^{*})}^{\mathbf{pd}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathrm{B}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{cAbs}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\Omega}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\widehat{\mathrm{B}}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Sub}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

where one starts with classical algebras over
\mathscr{P}
, and where divided powers appear in all the other
\infty
-categories in the square. Moreover, if one starts with divided powers algebras over
\mathscr{P}
, there should be another inclusion-restriction square of the form

(14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathscr{P}}^{\mathbf{pd}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P})}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)),}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathscr{P}^{*}}^{\mathbf{pd}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbf{cobar}(\mathscr{P}^{*})}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\mathrm{B}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{cAbs}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\Omega}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\widehat{\mathrm{B}}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bm{\widehat{\Omega}}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Sub}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

Defining all of these
\infty
-categories and constructing such adjunctions is beyond the scope of the present paper. Let us simply explain how these two variants can be observed at the level of point-set models.

In positive characteristic, one could choose to construct a point-set inclusion-restriction square, like in Theorem
2.11
, starting with a
quasi-planar
conilpotent dg cooperad
\mathcal{C}
as defined in
[
28
]
. Then, bottom horizontal adjunction in the aforementioned theorem would be a well-defined Quillen adjunction by
[
28
, Section 6]
. This Quillen adjunction should induce the bottom horizontal adjunction (
14
), since dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras up to quasi-isomorphisms encode
\infty
-categories of (non-necessarily conilpotent) coalgebras
without
divided powers by the rectification result of
[
48
, Theorem C]
. On the top horizontal adjunctions of Theorem
2.11
, one would have algebras over the dg operad
\mathcal{C}^{*}
, which is now injective as an dg symmetric sequence since
\mathcal{C}
is projective as a dg symmetric sequence. Therefore dg
\mathcal{C}^{*}
-algebras encode an
\infty
-category of divided powers algebras over an operad, see
[
15
]
for more on this type of algebras and their properties. However, dg
\mathcal{C}^{*}
-algebras a priori only have a semi-model structure, and it is not clear how to construct the top horizontal adjunction in (
14
) at the point-set level.

Another variant would be to start construct the inclusion-restriction square in Theorem
2.11
starting with a cofibrant dg operad
\mathcal{P}
. Then dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras up to quasi-isomorphism would encode an
\infty
-category of classical algebras over its underlying
\infty
-operad since any cofibrant operad is projective as a dg symmetric sequence. In this context, the top horizontal adjunction of (
13
) should be related to the Quillen adjunction constructed in
[
28
, Section 4]
in a similar way to what has been done in Section
3
. However, in this case
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
would be injective as a dg symmetric sequence and thus encode, in principle, an
\infty
-category of (non-necessarily conilpotent) divided powers coalgebras. However, in this case, we do not have a rectification result in the sense of
[
48
]
because this dg operad would not be cofibrant as an operad. So constructing point-set models for the bottom horizontal adjunction in (
13
) seems for the moment out of reach.

Remark 5.10
.

However
[
15
, Theorem 4.42]
shows that, up to composing with the linear dual functor, one has point-set models for the linear dual of the bar construction as a
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebra, which are divided powers algebras at the homotopical level.

6.
Derived Koszul equivalences and good completions

In this section, we define homotopy (co)completeness at the point-set level and we obtain an explicit point-set description of the equivalence in
[
36
, Theorem 2.1]
. We then discuss Heuts’s notion of an operad having good completions (meaning that trivial algebras are homotopy complete) and we define the dual notion of a cofibrant operad having good cocompletions. We finally put together all our previous constructions to obtain several fully faithful functors in different directions. We also give counterexamples to some natural-sounding statements which however are “too good to be true”.

6.1.
Homotopy (co)complete (co)algebras at the point-set level

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumptions
1
. We recall the definition of a homotopy complete dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra, already considered in
[
29
,
16
,
53
]
. We introduce the dual definition of a homotopy cocomplete dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebra. We show that these two definitions are in fact point-set descriptions of the notions introduced by
[
36
]
of
nilcomplete
algebras and
conilcomplete
coalgebras. Almost tautologically, the
\infty
-categorical bar-cobar adjunction
\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{P}}\dashv\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{P}}
restricts to an equivalence between these two
\infty
-subcategories, which reproves
[
36
, Theorem 2.1]
where the ambient stable
\infty
-category is
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
.

6.1.1.
Homotopy completeness

Let us consider the restriction adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c\mathrm{Abs}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

which is a Quillen adjunction by
Lemma

2.10
. Here we are considering the model structures where weak equivalences are given by quasi-isomorphisms on both sides.

Definition 6.1

(Homotopy complete dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra)
.

Let
A
be a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. It is
homotopy complete
if the derived unit of adjunction

\mathbb{L}\eta_{A}:A\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathrm{Res}\circ\mathbb{L}c\mathrm{Abs}(A)

is a quasi-isomorphism.

Remark 6.2
.

The idea behind this definition is that the underived adjunction unit
\mathrm{Res}\circ c\mathrm{Abs}
universally turns a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra to a complete
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebra, or “absolute” algebra. When
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{C}om
, this notion of completeness coincides with the
I
-adic completion of augmented commutative algebras in the finitely generated case, and it is in fact better behaved than the
I
-adic completion in the general case. See
[
53
, Section 3.4.1]
for more details. Coming back to the above definition, it is only natural to call a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
homotopy
complete in this sense when the
derived
unit of adjunction is an equivalence.

Remark 6.3
.

With our point-set models, we can make the condition of homotopy completeness extremely explicit on the point-set level. If
A
is a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra, then the standard proof that
\Omega\mathrm{B}A\to A
is a quasi-isomorphism uses that (disregarding the differential) we may write

\Omega\mathrm{B}A=\bigoplus_{n>0}(\mathcal{P}\circ\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P})(n)\otimes_{\mathbb S_{n}}A^{\otimes n}.

Now the chain complex
(\mathcal{P}\circ\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P})(n)
is acyclic for
n>1
. It follows that there are evident filtrations with respect to which
\Omega\mathrm{B}A\to A
is a filtered quasi-isomorphism, which gives the result since the filtrations are bounded below and exhaustive.
The condition of homotopy completeness of
A
is now that the natural embedding of

\Omega\mathrm{B}A=\bigoplus_{k>0}\mathcal{P}(k)\otimes_{\mathbb S_{k}}\bigoplus_{i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}=n}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{S}_{i_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{S}_{i_{k}}}^{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\left(\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}(i_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}(i_{k})\right)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}A^{\otimes n}

into

\widehat{\Omega}\mathrm{B}A=\prod_{k>0}\mathcal{P}(k)\otimes_{\mathbb S_{k}}\bigoplus_{i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}=n}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{S}_{i_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{S}_{i_{k}}}^{\mathbb{S}_{n}}\left(\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}(i_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}(i_{k})\right)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{n}}A^{\otimes n}

is a quasi-isomorphism. It is still a filtered quasi-isomorphism, for the same filtration as considered before, but this filtration is no longer exhaustive on the target, and so the filtered quasi-isomorphism is not necessarily a quasi-isomorphism. In favorable situations homotopy completeness can be proven by introducing further filtrations, as in
[
16
, Section 5]
.

Remark 6.4
.

By definition, the derived complete absolution functor
\mathbb{L}c\mathrm{Abs}
restricts to a fully faithful functor

\mathbb{L}c\mathrm{Abs}:\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{ho.comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\hookrightarrow\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]

between the
\infty
-subcategory of homotopy complete dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras up to quasi-isomorphisms and the
\infty
-category of complete dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebras up to quasi-isomorphisms.

Let
\mathscr{P}
be a enriched
\infty
-operad. Following
[
36
, Section 10]
, a
\mathscr{P}
-algebra
X
is
nilcomplete
if the canonical map

X\longrightarrow\lim_{k}\mathbf{t}_{k}X\penalty\ ,

is an equivalence, where
\mathbf{t}_{k}X
is the truncation of
X
at operations in arities bigger than
n
. The functors
\mathbf{t}_{n}(-)
are obtained by considering the successive arity truncations of the enriched
\infty
-operad
\mathscr{P}
.

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumptions
1
. Let
A
be a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. It is homotopy complete if and only if it is nilcomplete.

Proof.

This result follows directly from
Theorem

5.8
, since these two definitions are in fact two different descriptions of the unit of the
\infty
-categorical bar-cobar adjunction
\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{P}}\dashv\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{P}}
. Indeed, on the one hand, the nilcompletion map is shown to be the unit of this adjunction in
[
36
,
Proposition 10.4]
. On the other hand, let
A
be a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. It is homotopy complete if the derived unit

\mathbb{L}\eta_{A}:A\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\mathrm{Res}\circ\mathbb{L}c\mathrm{Abs}(A)

is a quasi-isomorphism. Using the canonical cofibrant resolution
\Omega_{\pi}\mathrm{B}_{\pi}(A)
of
A
, we compute that this derived unit is represented by the map

A\longrightarrow\mathrm{Res}\circ\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}\circ\mathrm{Inc}\circ\mathrm{B}_{\pi}(A)\penalty\ ,

and this also represents the (derived) unit of the
\infty
-categorical operadic bar-cobar adjunction
\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{P}}\dashv\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{P}}
. Hence the two notions coincide.
∎

Remark 6.6

(An explicit comparison with
nilcompletenes
)
.

There is actually a more illuminating way to understand Proposition
6.5
. From the results of Subsection
4.2
, we get a commuting triangle of Quillen adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 18.0pt\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip 42.0pt\lim_{k\geq 1}\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((i_{k})_{!}(-))_{k\geq 1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c\mathrm{Abs}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((i_{k})_{!}(-))_{k\geq 1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{k\geq 1}(i_{k})^{*}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{k\geq 1}(i_{k})^{*}(-)}

where left adjoints are drawn on the top and on the right, and where the vertical right adjunction is the one constructed in Subsection
4.2
. Passing to derived functors, the first triangle of Quillen adjunctions gives the following commutative triangle of units of adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Res}\circ\mathbb{L}c\mathrm{Abs}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lim_{k\in\mathbb{N}}^{h}(i_{k})^{*}\mathbb{L}(i_{k})_{!}(A)\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{3}}

for every dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
A
. The map
\eta_{1}:A\longrightarrow\mathrm{Res}\circ\mathbb{L}c\mathrm{Abs}(A)
is the map from
A
to its homotopy completion. The map
\eta_{3}:A\longrightarrow\lim_{k\in\mathbb{N}}^{h}(i_{k})^{*}\mathbb{L}(i_{k})_{!}(A)
is a model for the map from
A
to its nilcompletion. Finally, the map
\eta_{2}
is given by applying
\mathrm{Res}
to the unit of the adjunction in Theorem
4.4
at the object
\mathbb{L}c\mathrm{Abs}(A)
, so it is a natural equivalence, again by Theorem
4.4
. So indeed,
A
is homotopy complete if and only if it is nilcomplete.

6.1.2.
Homotopy cocompleteness.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be an dg operad satisfying Assumptions
1
. Let us consider the inclusion adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Sub}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

which is a Quillen adjunction by Lemma
2.10
. Here we are considering the model structures where weak equivalences are given by quasi-isomorphisms on both sides.

Definition 6.7

(Homotopy cocomplete dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebra)
.

Let
C
be a dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebra. It is
homotopy cocomplete
if the derived counit of the adjunction

\mathbb{R}\epsilon_{C}:\mathrm{Inc}\circ\mathbb{R}\mathrm{Sub}(C)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}C

is a quasi-isomorphism.

The idea of this definition is that dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras are inherently conilpotent (hence cocomplete) since by definition any element in them can only have finite iterated decompositions.
Homotopy
cocompleteness is the analogous notion at the derived level.

Remark 6.8
.

By definition, the derived maximal conilpotent subcoalgebra
\mathbb{R}\mathrm{Sub}
restricts to a fully faithful functor

\mathbb{R}\mathrm{Sub}:\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}^{\mathsf{ho.cocomp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\hookrightarrow\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]

between the
\infty
-subcategory of homotopy cocomplete dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras up to quasi-isomorphisms and the
\infty
-category of dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras up to quasi-isomorphisms.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be an enriched
\infty
-cooperad. Following
[
36
, Section 10]
, a (non-necessarily conilpotent)
\mathscr{C}
-coalgebra
Y
is
conilcomplete
if the canonical map

\lim_{k}\mathbf{t}_{k}C\longrightarrow C\penalty\ ,

is an equivalence, where
\mathbf{t}_{n}C
is the truncation of
C
at operations in arities bigger than
n
and is obtained by considering the successive arity truncations of the enriched
\infty
-cooperad
\mathscr{C}
.

Proposition 6.9
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumptions
1
. Let
C
be a dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebra. It is homotopy cocomplete if and only if it is conilcomplete.

Proof.

The proof is completely dual to that of Proposition
6.5
.
∎

Remark 6.10

(A more relaxed version of conilcompleteness)
.

Homotopy cocomplete dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras fully faithfully embed into the
strict
version of conilpotent coalgebras, enconded by the model category of dg coalgebras over the cooperad
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
up to quasi-isomorphisms. One could also define a more relaxed version of this notion asking the counit of the
\infty
-categorical adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Sub}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

to be an equivalence. Since dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras up to quasi-isomorphisms do not rectify conilpotent coalgebras over the underlying
\infty
-cooperad of
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
, this gives, in general, a different notion of conilcompleteness. See Counterexample
6.27
for an example where this notion disagrees with the definition of conilcompletess given earlier.

6.2.
The equivalence induced by the
\infty
-categorical bar-cobar adjunction.

One of the main results of
[
36
]
, Theorem 2.1, establishes an equivalence of
\infty
-categories between nilcomplete
\mathscr{P}
-algebras and conilcomplete
\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P})
-coalgebras. This theorem is viewed as a replacement for the original conjecture of Francis–Gaitsgory in
[
22
]
, which is disproved by Heuts. In our framework, the
\infty
-categorical bar-cobar adjunction restricts almost tautologically to an equivalence between homotopy complete
\mathcal{P}
-algebras and homotopy cocomplete
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras (which under Assumptions
1
, can be identified with non-conilpotent
\mathbf{bar}(\mathscr{P})
-coalgebras), and thus we recover, from the point-set descriptions introduced so far
[
36
, Theorem 2.1]
in the case where
\mathbf{C}=\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
.

Theorem 6.11
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumptions
1
. The adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

restricts to an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{ho.comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\simeq\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}^{\mathsf{ho.cocomp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\penalty\ ,

between the
\infty
-subcategory of homotopy complete dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebras and the
\infty
-subcategory of homotopy cocomplete dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras.

Proof.

As already explained in the proofs of Propositions
6.5
and
6.9
, the unit and the counit of the
\infty
-categorical bar-cobar adjunction can easily be identified with the maps that define homotopy completeness and homotopy cocompleteness, hence the result follows.
∎

6.3.
Operads with good (co)completions

In this subsection, we recall the definition of an operad with good completions introduced in
[
36
, Section 13]
.

Definition 6.12

(Operad with good completion)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be an dg operad. It has
good completion
if the for any chain complex
V
, the dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra given by
V
with the trivial algebra structure is homotopy complete.

The point-set description of homotopy complete algebras given in
Remark

6.3
can be made even more explicit here. Let
V
be a trivial
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. Taking the completion of the map
\Omega\mathrm{B}V\to V
gives
\widehat{\Omega}\mathrm{B}V\to\widehat{V}=V
(since a trivial algebra is in particular complete), which implies that
\mathcal{P}
has good completions if and only if the projection from

\widehat{\Omega}\mathrm{B}V=\prod_{k>0}\mathcal{P}(k)\otimes_{\mathbb S_{k}}\bigoplus_{i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}=d}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{S}_{i_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{S}_{i_{k}}}^{\mathbb{S}_{d}}\left(\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}(i_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}(i_{k})\right)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{d}}V^{\otimes d}

to the summand

\mathcal{P}(1)\otimes\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}(1)\otimes V=V

is a quasi-isomorphism, for any chain complex
V
.

The main example of operads with good completions are
Koszul operads
in the sense of the following definition.

Definition 6.13
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumptions
1
. We say that
\mathcal{P}
is
Koszul
if
H_{*}(\mathcal{P})=0
for
\ast\neq 0
, and
H_{*}(\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}(n))=0
for
\ast\neq n-1
.

Remark 6.14
.

The definition given here is somewhat restrictive: it covers the standard examples of
\mathcal{A}ss
,
\mathcal{C}om
, and
\mathcal{L}ie
, but not e.g.
\mathcal{P}ois_{n}
which is usually considered as a Koszul operad.

The following theorem is proven in
[
36
, Section 13.2]
, and by a somewhat different argument in
[
23
, Theorem 2.9.4]
.

Theorem 6.15

(Gaitsgory–Rozenblyum, Heuts)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a Koszul operad. Then
\mathcal{P}
has good completions.

Proof.

For the reader’s convenience, let us translate Heuts’s proof to our context. Koszulness implies
\mathcal{P}\simeq H_{*}(\mathcal{P})
and
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\simeq H_{*}(\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P})
, so we may and will replace both
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
with their homologies in what follows, and assume them both to be concentrated in a single degree. Consider the Koszul complex

K(d):=\bigoplus_{k\geq 0}\mathcal{P}(k)\otimes_{\mathbb S_{k}}\bigoplus_{i_{1}+\dots+i_{k}=d}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{S}_{i_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{S}_{i_{k}}}^{\mathbb{S}_{d}}\left(\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}(i_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}(i_{k})\right)

with its Koszul differential. It has a descending filtration by the arity of
\mathcal{P}
, with graded pieces

\operatorname{Gr}_{F}^{p}K(d)=\mathcal{P}(p)\otimes_{\mathbb S_{p}}\bigoplus_{i_{1}+\dots+i_{p}=d}\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{S}_{i_{1}}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{S}_{i_{p}}}^{\mathbb{S}_{d}}\left(\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}(i_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}(i_{p})\right).

By Koszulness,
\operatorname{Gr}_{F}^{p}K(d)
is concentrated in degree
d-p
. It follows that for each
p
, the complexes
F^{p}K(d)
and
K(d)/F^{p}K(d)
are simply the “brutal” truncations of
K(d)
. If
d>1
, the Koszul complex
K(d)
is acyclic, so its brutal truncations have homology concentrated in a single degree. In particular,
H_{*}(K(d)/F^{p}K(d))
is concentrated in degree
d-p
.

But now
\Omega\mathrm{B}V=\bigoplus_{d}K(d)\otimes_{\mathbb S_{d}}V^{\otimes d}
, and

\displaystyle\widehat{\Omega}\mathrm{B}V

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{p}\bigoplus_{d}K(d)/F^{p}K(d)\otimes_{\mathbb S_{d}}V^{\otimes d}

\displaystyle=K(1)\otimes V\oplus\varprojlim_{p}\bigoplus_{d>1}K(d)/F^{p}K(d)\otimes_{\mathbb S_{d}}V^{\otimes d}.

Since
K(d)/F^{p}K(d)
and
K(d)/F^{p-1}K(d)
have homology concentrated in different degrees for
d>1
, the map between them is nullhomotopic, and then all the maps in the above cofiltered system are nullhomotopic. So the inverse limit vanishes, and
\mathcal{P}
has good completions.
∎

It appears that there is no example in the literature of a dg operad which does
not
have good completions. Let us record one here.

Counter-Example 6.16
.

Consider the cooperad
\mathcal{C}_{0}
, whose underlying symmetric sequence is given by
\mathcal{C}_{0}(n)=\Bbbk.c_{n}
concentrated in degree
0
for all
n\geq 1
, with the trivial
\mathbb{S}_{n}
-action and zero differential, and all cocompositions identically zero. Then the dg operad
\mathcal{P}=\Omega\mathcal{C}_{0}
(which is simply the free dg operad on the symmetric sequence
s^{-1}\mathcal{C}_{0}
) does not have good completion.

Let us explain why. Consider
V=\Bbbk.v
, a 1-dimensional vector space over
\Bbbk
, viewed as a trivial dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}_{0}
-algebra concentrated in degree
0
. We use the bar-cobar adjunction relative to the Koszul twisting morphism
\iota:\mathcal{C}_{0}\longrightarrow\Omega\mathcal{C}_{0}
to obtain a cofibrant resolution of
V
. Using this resolution, we deduce that
V
is a homotopy complete dg
\Omega\mathcal{C}_{0}
-algebra if and only if the map

\iota_{V}:\bigoplus_{k\geq 1}\Omega\mathcal{C}_{0}(k)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{k}}(\mathcal{C}_{0}\circ V)^{\otimes k}\longrightarrow\prod_{k\geq 1}\Omega\mathcal{C}_{0}(k)\otimes_{\mathbb{S}_{k}}(\mathcal{C}_{0}\circ V)^{\otimes k}

is a quasi-isomorphism. However, this is not the case. Indeed, on the right hand side, the formal power series

\alpha=\sum_{k\geq 1}s^{-1}c_{k}\circ(c_{1},\dots,c_{1})(v,\dots,v)\penalty\ ,

is a degree
-1
element which closed, which is not in the image of the differential, and which cannot be in the image of the inclusion
\iota_{V}
. So the map
\iota_{V}
is not a quasi-isomorphism.

6.3.1.
Operads with good cocompletion.

We now define, for cofibrant dg operads, the notion of having good cocompletions. Notice that, up to equivalence, we can always choose an dg operad of the form
\Omega\mathcal{C}
, for some conilpotent dg cooperad
\mathcal{C}
. Moreover, given Assumptions
1
, we can always choose it of the form
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
, for some dg operad
\mathcal{P}
satisfying Assumptions
1
.

Definition 6.17

(Operad with good cocompletion)
.

Let
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
be a cofibrant dg operad. It has
good cocompletion
if the for any chain complex
V
, the dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebra given by
V
with the trivial coalgebra structure is homotopy cocomplete.

We want to understand when
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
has good cocompletion, meaning that trivial dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
are homotopy cocomplete. The amounts to understanding when the canonical map

\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P}^{*})(V)\rightarrowtail\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P}^{*})(V)

is a quasi-isomorphism for any chain complex
V
. The complex
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P}^{*})(V)
involves the cofree construction, which quite hard to compute, so we start by reducing this question to a more manageable one.

Lemma 6.18
.

A dg operad
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
has good cocompletion if and only if the canonical inclusion

\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\mathrm{S}(\mathcal{P}^{*})(V)\rightarrowtail\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P}^{*})(V)

is a quasi-isomorphism for all chain complexes
V
.

Proof.

Consider the following commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P}^{*})(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P}^{*})(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\mathrm{S}(\mathcal{P}^{*})(V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

in chain complexes. The vertical right and bottom horizontal arrow are quasi-isomorphisms, therefore the diagonal map is also a quasi-isomorphism. Thus, by 2-out-of-3, the vertical left inclusion is a quasi-isomorphism if and only if the horizontal top one is.
∎

Remark 6.19
.

Yet another way to phrase things is to say that the canonical map

\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P}^{*})(V)\longrightarrow V

is a quasi-isomorphism.

The first, quite obvious example that we have of a cofibrant dg operad with good cocompletion is the case of cobar constructions of truncated cooperads.

Proposition 6.20
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumptions
1
. Then the dg operad
\Omega(\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{P}^{*})
has good cocompletion for all
k\geq 1
.

Proof.

In this case, the map

\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\mathrm{S}(\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{P})(V)\rightarrowtail\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{P}^{*})(V)

is in fact an isomorphism for any
k\geq 1
, hence we can conclude by Lemma
6.18
.
∎

This property seems to be much more rare in nature than operads with good completion, and in fact basic examples of cofibrant dg operads fail to have good cocompletion.

Counter-Example 6.21
.

Let
\Omega\mathcal{C}om^{*}
be the dg operad encoding
\mathcal{L}_{\infty}
-(co)algebras. Then
\mathcal{P}
does
not
have good cocompletion. Indeed, let
V
be a countable infinite dimensional vector space concentrated in degree
0
. Then we have that

\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{C}om^{*})(V)\cong\Bbbk\llbracket x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},\dots\rrbracket\penalty\ ,

that is, the commutative algebra of formal power series in a countable infinite number of variables. Then it turns out that the map

\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\left(\Bbbk\llbracket x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},\dots\rrbracket\right)\longrightarrow V

is not a quasi-isomorphism. This is because, while formal power series rings in a
finite
number of variables are smooth over the base field, it is no longer the case for formal power series in an
infinite
number of variables. See
[
23
, Remark 2.6.7]
for more details on this counter-example. Notice that formal power series rings in infinitely many variables are also used by Heuts in
[
36
, Lemma 12.3]
as a counter-example to the original Francis–Gaitsgory conjecture, since they are
I
-adic completions which are not themselves
I
-adically complete.

The relationship between these two phenomena is the following: if the dg operad
\Omega\mathcal{C}om^{*}
had good completion, then we would have an equivalence of
\infty
-categories by Proposition
6.28

\mathcal{C}om^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\simeq\mathcal{C}om\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{ho.comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\penalty\ ,

between (complete) dg absolute commutative algebras up to quasi-isomorphisms and homotopy complete dg commutative algebras up to quasi-isomorphisms. Rectricting to degree
0
(discrete objects) this equivalence would give an equivalence of 1-categories between absolute commutative algebras and
I
-adically completed commutative algebras, which cannot exist because the free absolute commutative algebra on an infinite number of variables
\Bbbk\llbracket x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},\dots\rrbracket
is not
I
-adically complete. See also Remark
2.5
and
[
53
, Section 3.4]
for more details.

6.4.
Derived inclusions and equivalences

In this subsection, we lay out the different fully faithful functors between the different
\infty
-categories that we have considered so far, and we explain why most of them are not in general equivalences of
\infty
-categories. We also explain some of the consequences of assuming that an operad has good (co)completions in this context.

Proposition 6.22
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
a dg operad satisfying Assumptions
1
. There are the following fully faithful functors of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{ho.comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}^{\mathsf{ho.cocomp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}c\mathrm{Abs}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathrm{Sub}}

where the top left and the bottom right are equivalent via the
\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{C}}\dashv\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{C}}
adjunction.

Proof.

The functors are those of Theorem
2.11
and the fact that the top left and the bottom right
\infty
-categories are equivalent is given by Theorem
6.11
. The two vertical functors are fully faithful by definition of homotopy complete
\mathcal{C}^{*}
-algebras and homotopy cocomplete
\Omega\mathcal{C}
-coalgebras.

Let us show that the functor
\mathrm{B}_{\pi}
is fully faithful when restricted to homotopy complete
\mathcal{P}
-algebras. It is fully faithful (in fact, an equivalence) when it lands in the
\infty
-category of dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras up to transferred weak equivalences, of which the
\infty
-category of dg
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras up to quasi-isomorphisms is a localization. So it suffices to show that if
A
is a homotopy complete
\mathcal{P}
-algebra, then
\mathrm{B}_{\pi}A
is a local object with respect to quasi-isomorphism. There are weak equivalences of mapping spaces

\displaystyle\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{W.eq}^{-1}]}(-,\mathrm{B}_{\pi}(A))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}(\Omega_{\pi}(-),A)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}(\Omega_{\pi}(-),\mathrm{Res}\penalty\ \mathbb{L}c\mathrm{Abs}A)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}(\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}(\mathrm{Inc}(-)),\mathbb{L}c\mathrm{Abs}A)\penalty\ ,

and this last mapping space clearly sends quasi-isomorphisms to weak equivalences of mapping spaces, since both
\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}
and
\mathrm{Inc}
preserve quasi-isomorphisms. Hence
\mathrm{B}_{\pi}A
is indeed local with respect to quasi-isomorphisms and the functor
\mathrm{B}_{\pi}
is fully faithful. The proof for
\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}
is entirely analogous.
∎

Counter-Example 6.23
.

The fully faithful functor

\mathrm{B}_{\pi}:\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{ho.comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\longrightarrow\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]

is not, in general, an equivalence of
\infty
-categories. Indeed, if it were, then the functor

\mathrm{Inc}:\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\longrightarrow\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]

would necessarily have to be fully faithful as well. Now, take
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{C}om
, and consider an infinite-dimensional vector space
V
, concentrated in degree
0
, endowed with the trivial
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebra structure. The derived counit

\mathbb{R}\mathrm{Sub}\penalty\ \mathrm{Inc}(V)\longrightarrow V

can be computed to be the map

\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\left(\Bbbk\llbracket V\rrbracket\right)\longrightarrow V

which, as explained in Counterexample
6.21
, is not a quasi-isomorphism.

6.4.1.
The case of operads with good completions.

If we put together Theorem
2.11
with the commutative triangle of Proposition
5.5
and Theorem
3.4
, we get the following diagram of adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\penalty\ \mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{B}_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\Omega_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{loose}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Inc}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{strict}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Sub}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathrm{Sub}}

where, once again, left adjoints are written on top. We state, with our notation, the following key result.

Proposition 6.24

(
[
36
, Proposition 11.1]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisying Assumptions
1
. Assume that
\mathcal{P}
has good completions and let
A
be a dg
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. Then the comparison map

\bm{\Omega}^{\mathbf{enh}}\bm{\mathrm{B}}^{\mathbf{enh}}(A)\longrightarrow\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{P}}\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{P}}(A)

is an equivalence in the
\infty
-category of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras.

Corollary 6.25
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisying Assumptions
1
with good completions. The functor

\mathbf{loose}:\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\longrightarrow\mathbf{Coalg}_{\penalty\ \mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))

is fully faithful on the essential image of homotopy complete
\mathcal{P}
-algebras via the operadic bar construction
\mathrm{B}_{\pi}
.

Proof.

Let
A
be a homotopy complete
\mathcal{P}
-algebra. Then, by definition, the map

A\longrightarrow\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{P}}\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{P}}(A)\simeq\mathbb{R}\Omega_{\pi}\penalty\ \mathbb{R}\mathrm{Sub}\penalty\ \mathrm{Inc}\penalty\ \mathrm{B}_{\pi}(A)

is an equivalence. Hence, by Proposition
6.24
, the map

A\longrightarrow\bm{\Omega}^{\mathbf{enh}}\bm{\mathrm{B}}^{\mathbf{enh}}(A)\simeq\mathbb{R}\Omega_{\pi}\penalty\ \mathbf{strict}\penalty\ \mathbf{loose}\penalty\ \mathrm{B}_{\pi}(A)

is also an equivalence. Therefore, since
\mathrm{B}_{\pi}
is fully faithful on all homotopy complete
\mathcal{P}
-algebras, we deduce that
\mathbf{loose}
must also be fully faithful on the essential image of homotopy complete
\mathcal{P}
-algebras in
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-algebras via the functor
\mathrm{B}_{\pi}
.
∎

Example 6.26
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a connective dg operad. Then there are equivalences of
\infty
-categories

\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}_{\geq 1}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\simeq\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{cog}_{\geq 1}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\simeq\mathbf{Coalg}_{\penalty\ \mathbf{bar}(\mathcal{P})}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)))_{\geq 1}^{\mathbf{conil}}

between
\mathcal{P}
-algebras in degrees
\geq 1
,
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras in degrees
\geq 1
and conilpotent coalgebras over the enriched
\infty
-cooperad
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
in degrees
\geq 1
. In particular, on these sub-categories the rectification of conilpotent
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
-coalgebras holds. This can be interpreted as an example of Corollary
6.25
. Indeed, since
\mathcal{P}
is connective, any
\mathcal{P}
-algebra in degrees
\geq 1
is homotopy complete. An example of this are dg Lie algebras in degrees
\geq 1
appearing in the work of Quillen in rational homotopy
[
50
]
.

Counter-Example 6.27
.

As already pointed out in
[
18
, Theorem 5.3.1]
, the adjunction
\mathbf{loose}\dashv\mathbf{strict}
is not, in general, an equivalence. Let us give yet another reinterpretation of the Counterexample
6.16
to illustrate this.

Let
\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{C}om
. Since
\mathcal{C}om
has good completions by Theorem
6.15
, it implies, by
[
36
, Remark 11.2]
, that the functor
\mathbf{Inc}
is fully faithful on trivial coalgebras over the enriched
\infty
-cooperad
\mathrm{B}\mathcal{P}
. This, in turn, means that the functor
\mathbf{Sub}
is also fully faithful on any trivial
\Omega\mathcal{C}om^{*}
-coalgebra. However, as we saw in Counterexample
6.16
, the operad
\Omega\mathcal{C}om^{*}
does not have good cocompletions because infinite dimensional trivial
\Omega\mathcal{C}om^{*}
-coalgebras in degree
0
are not homotopy cocomplete. This, in turn, means that
\mathbb{R}\mathrm{Sub}
is not fully faithful on infinite dimensional trivial
\Omega\mathcal{C}om^{*}
-coalgebras. Which, finally, implies that the functor
\mathbf{strict}
is not fully faithful either when restricted to infinite dimensional trivial coalgebras. Hence, this adjunction cannot be an equivalence.

6.4.2.
The case of operads with good cocompletions.

We show that when the cofibrant operad encoding coalgebras has good cocompletions, homotopy complete and absolute algebras on the other side of Koszul duality coincide.

Proposition 6.28
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisying Assumptions
1
. If the dg operad
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
has good cocompletions if and only if there are equivalences

\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{ho.comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\simeq\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\simeq\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}^{\mathsf{ho.cocomp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\penalty\ .

Proof.

The adjunction
\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}\dashv\mathbb{L}\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}
induces an equivalence at the level of
\infty
-categories if and only if, for any complete dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebra
B
, its image
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}(V)
is homotopy cocomplete. Indeed, this is beacause we already know have that
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}
is fully faithful.

First, we observe that
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
has good cocompletion precisely if, for any chain complex
V
, the canoncial map

\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P}^{*})(V)\longrightarrow\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P}^{*})(V)

is a quasi-isomorphism. That is, if and only if the image by
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}
of the free dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebra on
V
is homotopy cocomplete. Therefore, if the equivalences hold, then
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
has good cocompletion.

Now, in order to show that the image of any dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebra via this functor is homotopy cocomplete, we use that the
\infty
-category of complete dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebras up to quasi-isomorphisms is monadic over
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
by Corollary
4.7
, hence any algebra
B
can be written as the geometric realization of a
\mathrm{U}
-split simplicial diagram of free dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebras.

The image of this geometric realization by
\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}
is again homotopy cocomplete since we have that

\mathbb{R}\mathrm{Sub}\circ\widehat{\mathrm{B}}_{\iota}\simeq\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\circ\mathrm{Res}\penalty\ ,

where
\mathrm{Res}
preserves geometric realization of a
\mathrm{U}
-split simplicial diagrams because it commutes with the forgetful functors and where
\mathrm{B}_{\pi}
preserves all colimits since it is a left adjoint. Thus, the image of any dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebra
B
via the complete bar functor is again homotopy cocomplete and the equivalences hold.
∎

Remark 6.29

(Operads with good finite cocompletion)
.

As explained in Counterexample
6.21
, basic examples of cofibrant operads fail to have good cocompletions. However, one could introduce the notion of a dg operad
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
with
good finite cocompletion
, that is, such that the map

\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\mathrm{S}(\mathcal{P})(V)\rightarrowtail\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\widehat{\mathrm{S}}^{c}(\mathcal{P}^{*})(V)

is a quasi-isomorphism for all perfect chain complexes
V
. The fact that
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
has finite good cocompletion should, in principle, induce a equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}_{\mathsf{f.g}}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\simeq\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}_{\mathsf{f.g}}^{\mathsf{ho.comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]

between finitely generated complete dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebras up to quasi-isomorphisms and finitely generated homotopy complete dg
\mathcal{P}^{*}
-algebras up to quasi-isomorphisms. Notice that examples like
\Omega\mathcal{C}om^{*}
have good finite cocompletion but not good cocompletion.

6.4.3.
Truncated operads and point-set models for conilpotent coalgebras over their bar constructions.

Finally, we deal with arity-truncated dg operads and their bar constructions, and we show that the rectification of conilpotent coalgebras always holds in this case.

Proposition 6.30
.

Let
\mathcal{P}
be a dg operad satisfying Assumptions
1
. For any
k\geq 1
, the functor

\mathbf{loose}:\mathrm{B}(\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{P})\mbox{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\longrightarrow\mathbf{Coalg}_{\penalty\ \mathbf{bar}(\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{P})}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk))

is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories, where
\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{P}
is the truncation of
\mathcal{P}
at arity
k
.

Proof.

This essentially follows from the fact that, for any
k\geq 1
, the point-set cobar construction
\Omega_{\pi}
with respect to the twisting morphism
\pi:\mathrm{B}(\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{P})\longrightarrow\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{P}
preserves all quasi-isomorphisms. Hence the point-set bar-cobar adjunction induces is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{P}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\simeq\mathrm{B}(\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{P})\mbox{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\penalty\ ,

which combined with
[
36
, Proposition 13.13.]
, gives the desired result.
∎

Remark 6.31
.

Under these hypotheses, Proposition
6.28
also applies, so we also get that

\mathrm{B}(\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{P})\mbox{-}\mathsf{cog}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\simeq\tau^{\leq k}\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}^{\mathsf{comp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\simeq\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}^{\mathsf{ho.cocomp}}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]\penalty\ ,

hence all
\infty
-categories considered are in fact equivalent except for dg
\Omega\mathcal{P}^{*}
-coalgebras up to quasi-isomorphisms.

7.
Point-set models for bar-cobar adjunctions of
\mathbb E_{n}
-algebras à la Lurie

The goal of this section is to give point-set models for Lurie’s bar-cobar adjunction between augmented
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-algebras and coaugmented
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-coalgebras as well as its generalization to the
\mathbb{E}_{n}
case by Ayala–Francis, both in the case of chain complexes over a field of any characteristic.
Both of these functors appear as the
\infty
-categorical bar cobar adjunction of Theorem
2.16
when one takes
\mathcal{P}
to be a point-set model of the
\mathbb{E}_{n}
operad.

7.1.
Lurie’s bar-cobar adjunction and its generalization

In
[
43
, Section 4.3]
, Lurie constructed a universal adjunction between augmented monoids and coaugmented comonoids in any pointed monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathbf{D}
admitting geometric realizations of simplicial objects and totalizations of cosimplicial objects:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbb{E}_{1}}^{\mathbf{aug}}(\mathbf{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbb{E}_{1}}^{\mathbf{coaug}}(\mathbf{D})\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{bar}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{cobar}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

The idea behind this adjunction is the following: given an augmented monoid, one can construct canonically a simplicial object, whose realization is precisely the bar functor. Dually, given a coaugmented comonoid, one can construct canonically a cosimplicial object, whose totalization is precisely the cobar functor. See Subsection
1.2.2
for an example of this constructions in the case of
\infty
-operads and
\infty
-cooperads.

Later, Ayala and Francis constructed a generalization of this adjunction in
[
4
]
. We follow the exposition of
[
5
, Section 3.2]
. Suppose now that
\mathbf{D}
admits all sifted limits and colimits and that the monoidal structure distributes over both of them. Then, for any
n\geq 1
, there is a bar-cobar adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbb{E}_{n}}^{\mathbf{aug}}(\mathbf{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbb{E}_{n}}^{\mathbf{coaug}}(\mathbf{D})\penalty\ .}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{bar}^{(n)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{cobar}^{(n)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

between augmented
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-algebras in
\mathbf{D}
and coaugmented
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-coalgebras in
\mathbf{D}
.

7.2.
A homotopy-coherent comparison between two bar constructions

On first sight, one might be tempted to think that the above adjunctions coincide with the ones of Subsection
3.1
, which were constructed in
[
22
]
in the particular case of the
\mathbb{E}_{n}
operad. This cannot be the case, however, as the two categories of coalgebras on the right of these adjunctions are
not
the same. Indeed, in the enhanced bar-cobar adjunction, one has
conilpotent
coaugmented
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-coalgebras, whereas here one has
all
coaugmented
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-coalgebras.

The question then becomes how to compare these two adjunctions and the notions of Koszul duality that they induce. It can be seen that at the level of the underlying objects in
\mathbf{D}
, both bar constructions coincide up to a shift, see
[
13
, Section 4.1]
. However, we need a full comparison of the two functors as homotopy coherent
coalgebras
, a result which was recently obtained by Heuts and Land in the case when
\mathbf{D}
is the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Sp}
of spectra.

Theorem 7.1

(
[
34
]
)
.

For any
n\geq 1
, there is an essentially unique equivalence of
\infty
-operads

(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{E}_{n}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}s^{-n}\mathbb{E}_{n}^{\mathrm{nu}}

such that the following square of
\infty
-categories commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbb{E}_{n}^{\mathrm{nu}}}(\mathbf{Sp})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbb{E}_{n}^{\mathrm{nu}}}(\mathbf{Sp})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathrm{B}\mathbb{E}_{n}^{\mathrm{nu}}}^{\mathbf{conil}}(\mathbf{Sp})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{E}_{n}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\vee}}(\mathbf{Sp})\simeq\mathbf{Coalg}_{s^{-n}\mathbb{E}_{n}^{\mathrm{nu}}}(\mathbf{Sp})\penalty\ ,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{bar}^{(n)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{B}^{\mathbf{enh}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{inc}}

where the functor
\mathbf{inc}
is the inclusion of conilpotent coalgebras over the
\infty
-cooperad
\mathrm{B}\mathbb{E}_{n}^{\mathrm{nu}}
into all coalgebras over its Spanier-Whitehead dual
\infty
-operad
(\mathrm{B}\mathbb{E}_{n}^{\mathrm{nu}})^{\vee}
.

Remark 7.2
.

Notice that
augmented

\mathbb{E}_{n}
-algebras are canonically equivalent to algebras over the non-unital version
\mathbb{E}_{n}^{\mathrm{nu}}
of the
\mathbb{E}_{n}
operad, and similarly for coaugmented coalgebras.

Remark 7.3
.

The original result in
[
34
]
is stated using
\infty
-cooperads and the definition of non-conilpotent coalgebras over them given in
[
36
, Appendix A]
. We have slightly reformulated it in an equivalent way, using Lemma
5.1
for the convinience of the reader.

Remark 7.4
.

By smashing with the Eilenberg-MacLane spectrum
\mathrm{H}\Bbbk
, Theorem
7.1
also holds when we replace the
\infty
-category of spectra with the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{D}(\Bbbk)
.

7.3.
Point-set models for everyone

We construct concrete point-set models for Lurie’s bar-cobar adjunction in the base
\infty
-category of chain complexes over a field of any characteristic. We also construct point-set models for Ayala–Francis bar-cobar adjunctions between non-unital
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-algebras and non-counital
\mathbb{E}_{n}
-coalgebras over a field of characteristic zero when
n\geq 2
.

Theorem 7.5
.

Let
\Bbbk
be a field of any characteristic and let
\mathcal{A}ss
be the associative operad. The adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}ss\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{A}ss^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{A}ss}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{A}ss}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is naturally weakly equivalent to the following composite adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbb{E}_{1}}^{\mathbf{aug}}(\mathbf{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbb{E}_{1}}^{\mathbf{coaug}}(\mathbf{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{s^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{1}}^{\mathbf{coaug}}(\mathbf{D})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{bar}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{cobar}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

that is, to Lurie’s bar-cobar adjunction up to a shift.

Proof.

Recall that the existence of such an adjunction at the
\infty
-categorical level was proven in Theorem
2.16
, where these functors are given by the compositions

\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{A}ss}=\mathrm{Inc}\circ\mathrm{B}_{\pi}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{A}ss}=\mathrm{Res}\circ\widehat{\Omega}_{\iota}

in the commutative square of Theorem
2.11
.

First, we have to check that each of these
\infty
-categories presents, repectively, the
\infty
-categories of augmented
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-algebras and coaugmented
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-coalgebras. The fact that dg associative algebras localized at quasi-isomorphisms present their augmented
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-algebras follows from standard rectification arguments, see
[
43
, Section 4.1.8]
. Notice that the dg operad
\Omega\mathcal{A}ss^{*}
encodes (shifted)
\mathcal{A}_{\infty}
-(co)algebras, so this is where the shift appears. The fact that dg
\Omega\mathcal{A}ss^{*}
-coalgebras localized at quasi-isomorphisms present (shifted) coaugmented
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-coalgebras follows from Theorem
1.37
, which is the main result of
[
48
]
.

Hence we are left to compare the adjunctions, since their source and targets are equivalent. In order to do so, it suffices to show that the left adjoints are weakly equivalent. This follows directly from Theorem
7.1
combined with Theorem
3.4
and Proposition
5.5
.
∎

Theorem 7.6
.

Let
n\geq 2
and
\Bbbk
be a field of characteristic zero. Let
\mathcal{P}ois_{n}
denote the operad of
n
-Poisson algebras. The adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{P}ois_{n}\mbox{-}\mathsf{alg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega\mathcal{P}ois_{n}^{*}\mbox{-}\mathsf{coalg}\penalty\ [\mathsf{Q.iso}^{-1}]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{bar}_{\mathcal{P}ois_{n}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{cobar}_{\mathcal{P}ois_{n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}

is naturally weakly equivalent to the following composite adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Alg}_{\mathbb{E}_{n}}^{\mathbf{aug}}(\mathbf{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{\mathbb{E}_{n}}^{\mathbf{coaug}}(\mathbf{D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Coalg}_{s^{-n}\mathbb{E}_{n}}^{\mathbf{coaug}}(\mathbf{D})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{bar}^{(n)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{cobar}^{(n)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dashv}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{-n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

that is, to Ayala–Francis’s bar-cobar adjunction up to an iterated shift.
